The irreducibility of the moduli space of pointed stable curves with dormant PGL 2 ( N ) \mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)} -oper
Yasuhiro Wakabayashi

Abstract
. A PGL n ( N ) \mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)} -oper is a specific type of flat PGL n \mathrm{PGL}_{n} -bundle on an algebraic curve in prime characteristic p p enhanced by an action of the sheaf of differential operators of level N − 1 N-1 . In this paper, we introduce and study a higher-level generalization of the Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism on the moduli space of PGL n ( N ) \mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)} -opers, defined via the characteristic polynomials of their p N p^{N} -curvatures. As an application, we prove the irreducibility of the moduli space classifying pointed stable curves equipped with dormant PGL 2 ( N ) \mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)} -opers, i.e., PGL 2 ( N ) \mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)} -opers with vanishing p N p^{N} -curvature.

;

: Primary 14H60, Secondary 14G17;

Key words: dormant oper; moduli space; Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism;
p
-curvature; algebraic curve

1.
Introduction

A
G
-oper
for a reductive group
G
is a specific type of flat
G
-bundle on an algebraic curve.

G
-opers were originally introduced
in the study of the geometric Langlands correspondence for constructing Hecke eigensheaves on the moduli space of bundles via quantization of Hitchin’s integrable system (cf.
[
BeDr
]
).

For instance,

\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-opers (for
n\geq 1
) may be described as
flat vector bundles of rank
n
equipped with complete flags, corresponding locally to
scalar differential equations of the form

\displaystyle Dy=0,\ \ \ D=\frac{d^{n}}{dx^{n}}+a_{1}\frac{d^{n-1}}{dx^{n-1}}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\frac{d}{dx}+a_{n},

(1.1)

where
x
is
a local coordinate on the underlying curve and
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}
are coefficient functions.

In another important example,
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on a complex projective smooth curve
X

have been studied from the perspective of Teichmüller theory.
Indeed, if
X^{\mathrm{an}}
denotes the associated Riemann surface, then a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper determines (up to conjugation)
a representation of
the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X^{\mathrm{an}})\rightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, and
such representations encode
refinements of the complex structure of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
, known as

projective structures
.
This means that each
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper corresponds to an atlas of coordinate charts into the Riemann sphere
\hat{\mathbb{C}}:=\mathbb{C}\sqcup\{\infty\}
, with translation functions given by Möbius transformations.
A canonical example is the
uniformizing projective structure
, which arises from
the Fuchsian representation associated to the uniformization of
X^{\mathrm{an}}
.

G
-opers on a hyperbolic curve
in prime characteristic
p>0
have been studied in the context of a characteristic-
p
analogue of the geometric Langlands correspondence (cf.
[
BeTr
]
), as well as various other topics, including

p
-adic Teichmüller theory (cf., e.g.,
[
Moc1
]
,
[
Moc2
]
,
[
JRXY
]
,
[
JoPa
]
,
[
LasPa1
]
, and
[
LiOs
]
).
A key common feature in these developments is the notion of
p
-curvature of a connection, which serves as an
invariant measuring the obstruction to the compatibility of
p
-power structures that arise in certain spaces of infinitesimal symmetries.
This invariant also plays a central role in
the Grothendieck-Katz conjecture, which provides a conjectural criterion for
the algebraicity of solutions to linear differential equations
(cf.
[
NKa3
]
,
[
And
]
).

A
G
-oper is said to be
dormant
if its
p
-curvature vanishes.
For example, a
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-oper is dormant if and only if the corresponding differential equation admits a full set of solutions.
The moduli theory of dormant
G
-opers for general
G
has been developed in, e.g.,
[
Wak1
]
,
[
Wak2
]
,
[
Wak4
]
,

[
Wak6
]
,
[
Wak12
]
,
[
Wak13
]
, and
[
Wak14
]
.
In these works, we introduced a generalization of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-opers
using the sheaf of differential operators of finite level, as developed by P. Berthelot and C. Montagnon (cf.
[
Ber1
]
,
[
Ber2
]
,
[
Mon
]
).
These generalized objects are referred to as

dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
(cf.
[
Wak6
]
,
[
Wak14
]
).
In
[
Hos
, Theorem A]
(or
[
Wak10
, Theorem A, (i)]
), it was shown that dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers correspond bijectively to
F^{N}
-projective structures
(cf.
[
Hos
, Definition 3.1]
,
[
Wak10
, Definition 1.2.1]
).
Within the framework of Teichmuller theory in characteristic
p
,
these may be regarded as analogues of ‘‘nice” projective structures on hyperbolic Riemann surfaces, such as
those with real monodromy, including the uniformizing projective structures
*
*

*

The analogy between real-monodromy opers and dormant opers appears in several aspects of Teichmüler theory.
One such aspect is that both exhibit an Eichler-Shimura-type decomposition in the cohomology of symmetric products of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers. In the case of real-monodromy opers, this phenomenon arises via an extension of Faltings’ result on the
discreteness
of
the space of real-monodromy
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers (cf.
[
Fal
]
,
[
Wak10
]
). In the other, the decomposition is induced by the
generic étaleness
of the space of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers (cf.
[
Wak9
]
).
Another point of analogy lies in the
2
d TQFT mentioned in this Introduction.
Dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on a
3
-pointed projective line are completely determined by elements of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, called “radii” (i.e., eigenvalues of the residue matrices at the marked points), which satisfy a certain “triangle inequality” (cf.
[
Moc2
, Introduction, Theorem 1.3]
). On the other hand, real-monodromy
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers on a pair of pants are characterized by a triangle inequality for the hyperbolic lengths of the boundary components (cf.
[
Gol
]
).
See also
[
BeTr
, Remark A.11]
for a discussion of this analogy from a viewpoint related to
an analytic version of the geometric Langlands correspondence.

.

In the case of elliptic curves,
dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

and
F^{N}
-affine structures
, which are equivalent to each other (cf.
[
Hos2
, Theorem B, Definition 2.1]
,
[
Wak10
, Theorem A, (ii), Definition 1.2.1]
), play the role of their counterparts.
These are further described by
Igusa-structures of level
p^{N}
, which are, by definition, generators of the iterated Verschiebung (cf.
[
KaMa
, Definition 12.3.1]
).
Such a description allows us to interpret moduli spaces of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers as
higher-genus generalizations of the classical Igusa curves
(cf. Section
6
).

Now, we fix a positive integer
n
satisfying
1<n<p
,
an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p
, and a pair of nonnegative integers
(g,r)
satisfying
2g-2+r>0
.
Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
denote the moduli stack of
r
-pointed stable curves of genus
g
over
k
.
Given a positive integer
N
and an
r
-tuple
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
of elements of
a certain finite set
\Xi_{n,N}
(cf. (
3.14
)),
we define the moduli category

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r};\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\right)

(1.2)

that classifies pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})

consisting of a pointed curve
\mathscr{X}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
and a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
(resp., a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper; resp., a
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper)
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho
.
In particular, we have a sequence of inclusions

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\subseteq\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\subseteq\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}
.

According to
[
Wak14
, Theorem B]
,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
can be represented by a (possibly empty) proper Deligne-Mumford stack over
k
, and the projection
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
given by
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})\mapsto\mathscr{X}
is finite.
Moreover, if either “
n=2
” or “
N=1
and
2n<p
” is fulfilled, then this projection is generically étale (cf.
[
Wak4
, Theorem G]
,
[
Wak14
, Theorem C, (i)]
).
These geometric properties play crucial roles in establishing a factorization rule of
the total number of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers on a general curve according to various gluing procedures for underlying curves.
This factorization satisfies a
2
d TQFT, i.e., a
2
-dimensional topological quantum field theory (cf.
[
Wak14
, Theorem C, (ii)]
).
In the special case
N=1
, this
2
d TQFT is closely related to the Gromov-Witten theory of Grassmannian varieties and to the conformal field theory for affine Lie algebras (cf.
[
Wak4
]
).

The generic étaleness for
n=2
also gives rise to a certain arithmetic lifting of each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper to characteristic
p^{N}
, called the
canonical diagonal lifting
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Theorem D]
).
Thus,
a detailed investigation of the moduli stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
serves as a bridge between the theory of opers (as well as of linear differential equations) in arithmetic settings and various other types of enumerative geometry, thereby offering a unified framework for their mutual development.

In this paper, we focus on and prove certain fundamental properties of the stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
, regarded as a characteristic-
p
analogue of the moduli space of Riemann surfaces (equipped with uniformizing projective structures), as well as a higher-genus generalization of Igusa curves.
Our central result is the
irreducibility of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
.
The irreducibility of
the moduli space of smooth projective curves over an arbitrary field
was established in
[
DeMu
]
via the contractibility of Teichmüller spaces.
Furthermore,
in the context of
p
-adic Teichmüller theory,
the same property for
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
with
N=1
has already been shown in
[
Moc2
, Chapter II, Theorem 2.8]

(cf.
[
KaMa
, Theorem 12.6.1, Corollary 12.6.2]
for the case of Igusa curves).

A key tool in our approach is a higher-level generalization of the so-called Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism, previously studied in
[
BeTr
]
,
[
LasPa1
]
,
[
JoPa
]
.
Using a certain relative affine space
B_{\mathrm{univ},0}^{\flat}
over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(cf. (
3.95
)),
we construct a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}_{\widetilde{\rho}}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\rightarrow B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ},0},

(1.3)

for each
\widetilde{\rho}\in\Xi_{n,N+1}^{r}\left(=\Xi_{n,N+1}\times\cdots\times\Xi_{n,N+1}\right)
lifting
\rho
(cf. (
3.102
)),
defined by taking the characteristic polynomials of the
p^{N}
-curvatures of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-opers.
We refer to this morphism as the
level-
N
Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism
(cf. Definition
3.22
).

Under certain assumptions,
we prove that
this morphism satisfies some natural and desirable properties; see Theorem
3.21
for the precise statements.
Since
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}
can be obtained as the inverse image
of the zero section
0_{\mathrm{univ}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\rightarrow B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ},0}
,
the level-reduction morphism
\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}

turns out to be (finite, flat, and) surjective (cf. Theorem
3.23
).
By combining this fact
and the standard degeneration technique for underlying curves,
we establish some desired assertions (including the connectedness of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
) via induction on the dimension.
Our main results are summarized as follows.

Theorem A

(cf. Theorem
4.5
)
.

Suppose that
n=2
, and
let
\rho
be an element of
\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
, where we set
\rho:=\emptyset
when
r=0
.
Then, the following assertions hold:

(i)

If
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\neq\emptyset
,
then any two irreducible components of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
intersect.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is connected (when nonempty).

(ii)

If
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
,
then
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is irreducible.

In the above theorem, we assume the nonemptiness of the relevant moduli spaces.
For results concerning this property, we refer to Proposition
3.10
, Corollary
3.24
, and
[
Wak14
, Corollary 6.28, (ii), Proposition 10.18]
.

In Section
5
,
we present several direct applications of the irreducibility of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
.
These include
applications to
the generalized Verschiebung map on the moduli space of stable vector bundles (cf. Proposition
5.2
, Corollary
5.3
), and to certain arithmetic deformations
of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers (cf. Propositions
5.5
,
5.6
).
Regarding the former topic, we have also established an explicit and combinatorial procedure for computing the generic degree of the generalized Verschiebung map, based on the results of this paper (cf.
[
KoWa
]
).

Moreover, motivated by the present work, we intend to pursue further research from a coding-theoretic perspective
on the tower of stacks

\displaystyle\cdots\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho_{N},g,r}\xrightarrow{}\cdots\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{3,\rho_{2},g,r}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{2,\rho_{2},g,r}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,\rho_{1},g,r}.

(1.4)

(cf. (
4.71
))
formed by varying the level
N
(cf.
[
Wak15
]
).

Notation and Conventions

Throughout this paper,
all schemes (and Deligne-Mumford stacks) are assumed to be locally noetherian.

We fix
an odd prime
p
, an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p
, and
a positive integer
n
with
1<n<p
.

Let
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
(resp.,
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
) denote the general (resp., projective) linear group of
k^{\oplus n}
.
The algebraic group
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
contains
the Borel subgroup
B
consisting of the images of upper triangular invertible
n\times n
matrices under the natural quotient
\mathrm{GL}_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
The Lie algebra of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
(resp.,
B
) is denoted by

\mathfrak{g}:=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{PGL}_{n})
(resp.,
\mathfrak{b}:=\mathrm{Lie}(B)
).

Let
S
be a scheme (or more generally, a Deligne-Mumford stack).
We write
\mathcal{S}ch_{/S}
for the category of
S
-schemes.
For a vector bundle (i.e., a locally free coherent sheaf)
\mathcal{F}
on
S
, we denote by
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{F})
the relative affine scheme over
S
associated to
\mathcal{F}
, i.e., the spectrum
\mathcal{S}pec(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{V}^{\vee}))
of the symmetric algebra
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee})
of its dual
\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
over
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

Also, denote by
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F})\left(:=\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})\right)
the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear endomorphisms of
\mathcal{F}
,
and by
\overline{\mathcal{E}nd}(\mathcal{F})
the cokernel of the diagonal embedding
\mathcal{O}_{S}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F})
.
In particular, if
\mathcal{F}
has rank
n
, then
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F})
(resp.,
\overline{\mathcal{E}nd}(\mathcal{F})
) can be identified with the adjoint vector bundle associated to the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
-bundle (resp.,
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle) determined by
\mathcal{F}
.

2.
Preliminaries on
\mathcal{D}
-modules of finite level

In this section, we review basic materials on
\mathcal{D}
-modules of finite level in positive characteristic, including their logarithmic generalizations.
For detailed accounts, we refer the reader to

[
Ber1
]
,
[
Ber2
]
,
[
Mon
]
, and
[
Wak14
]
.

2.1.
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules in the logarithmic setting

Let
(g,r)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
2g-2+r>0
and

S
a scheme (or more generally, a Deligne-Mumford stack) over
k
.
Also, let
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
be an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
, where

f:X\rightarrow S
denotes the underlying prestable curve and
each
\sigma_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,r
) specifies the
i
-th marked point
S\rightarrow X
.

As shown in
[
FKa
, Theorem 2.6]
,
both
S
and
X
carry natural log structures.
We denote the resulting log structures by
S^{\mathrm{log}}
and
X^{\mathrm{log}}
, respectively.
The morphism
f
extends to a morphism of log schemes
f^{\mathrm{log}}:X^{\mathrm{log}}\rightarrow S^{\mathrm{log}}
, under which
X^{\mathrm{log}}
becomes a log curve over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
in the sense of
[
Wak14
, Definition 2.1]
.
We denote the sheaf of logarithmic
1
-forms on
X^{\mathrm{log}}
over
S^{\mathrm{log}}
by
\Omega_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
and its dual by

\mathcal{T}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.

Next, let

m
be a nonnegative integer.
Let
F_{S}
(resp.,
F_{X}
) denote the absolute Frobenius endomorphism of
S
(resp.,
X
).
We define
the
(m+1)
-st Frobenius twist
of
X
over
S
to be
the base-change
f^{(m+1)}:X^{(m+1)}\left(:=S\times_{F_{S}^{m+1},S}X\right)\rightarrow S
of the
S
-scheme
f:X\rightarrow S
along the
(m+1)
-st iterate
F_{S}^{m+1}
of
F_{S}
.
The associated morphism
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}\left(:=(f,F_{X}^{m+1})\right):X\rightarrow X^{(m+1)}
is called the
(m+1)
-st relative Frobenius morphism
of
X
over
S
.
The
i
-th marked point
\sigma_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,r
) determines a marked point
\sigma^{(m+1)}:S\rightarrow X^{(m+1)}
of
X^{(m+1)}
, and the resulting collection

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{(m+1)}:=(f^{(m+1)}:X^{(m+1)}\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}^{(m+1)}\}_{i=1}^{r})

(2.1)

forms an
r
-pointed stable curve.

To simplify the notation, we write
F_{X/S}:=F_{X/S}^{(1)}
, and write
\Omega^{(m+1)}:=\Omega_{X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
,

\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}:=\mathcal{T}_{X^{(m+1)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
.
Also, we occasionally write
X^{(0)}
,
f^{(0)}
,
\Omega^{(0)}
, and
\mathcal{T}^{(0)}
instead of
X
,
f
,
\Omega
, and
\mathcal{T}
, respectively, for notational convenience.

For each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we denote by

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq\ell}

the sheaf of logarithmic differential operators of level
m
and order
\leq\ell
,
as introduced in
[
Mon
, Définition 2.3.1]
.
We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}:=\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq\ell},

(2.2)

which is equipped with a structure of (non-commutative) algebras.
When there is no fear of confusion, we
abbreviate

\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}},\leq\ell}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\leq\infty}^{(m)}\ \left(\text{or simply}\ \mathcal{D}^{(m)}\right):=\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
.

Let
x
be a (locally defined) logarithmic coordinate on
X
(relative to
S
).
Associated to such a coordinate
x
, one has
a local
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-basis
\{\partial^{\langle j\rangle}\}_{j\leq\ell}
of
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Section 1.2.3]
).
For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, the pair of nonnegative integers
(q_{j},r_{j})
is uniquely determined by the condition
j=p^{m}\cdot q_{j}+r_{j}
with
0\leq r_{j}<p^{m}
.
Then,
the identity

\displaystyle\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\sum_{j=\mathrm{max}\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}^{j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}}\frac{j!}{(j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}-j)!\cdot(j-j^{\prime})!\cdot(j-j^{\prime\prime})!}\cdot\frac{q_{j^{\prime}}!\cdot q_{j^{\prime\prime}}!}{q_{j}!}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}

(2.3)

holds for any nonnegative integers
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
(cf.
[
Mon
, Lemme 2.3.4]
).
In particular,
the sections
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
satisfy the commutativity relation

\displaystyle\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\partial^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}.

(2.4)

Given each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
,
we shall write
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}
(resp.,
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}
) for the sheaf
\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}
endowed with a structure of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module arising from left (resp., right) multiplication by sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}^{(m)}\left(=\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
.
A
(left)
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
on an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
is defined as a left
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-action
\nabla:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
on
\mathcal{F}
extending its
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.
When it is necessary to emphasize the level “
m
”, we write
\nabla^{(m)}
instead of
\nabla
.
Note that giving such a structure for
m=0
amounts to giving an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
in the sense of
[
Wak4
, Definition 4.1]
, i.e., an
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear morphism
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}
satisfying the usual Leibnitz rule.
An
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module equipped with a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure (resp., an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection) is called a
(left)
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(resp., a
flat module
on
X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
).

For an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{F}
, we define the tensor product
\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}\otimes\mathcal{F}:={{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}\otimes\mathcal{F}
and equip it
with the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\leq\ell}^{(m)}
given by left multiplication.

Given a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)})

(2.5)

to be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{F}
on which
\mathcal{D}_{+}^{(m)}
acts as zero, where
\mathcal{D}_{+}^{(m)}
denotes the kernel of the canonical projection
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
The sheaf
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)})
can be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module via the underlying homeomorphism of
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
.

Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)})
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module.
For an integer
a
with
0\leq a\leq m
,
the composite

\displaystyle\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(a)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})

(2.6)

of
\nabla^{(m)}
and the natural morphism
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(a)}\rightarrow{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
defines a
\mathcal{D}^{(a)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
.
In particular, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(a)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)})
.
For an integer
a
with
0\leq a\leq m+1
, we
set

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{[a]}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}&\text{if $a=0$};\\
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a-1)})&\text{if $a>0$},\end{cases}

(2.7)

which is an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a)}}
-module.

In what follows, we define an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on the
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a)}}
-module
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
for
a=0,\cdots,m
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Section 2.6]
).
We begin by setting
\nabla^{[0]}
(or
(\nabla^{(m)})^{[0]}
)
:=\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(0)}
.
Now, let us fix an integer
a\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
.
Since

\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}-1}^{(a)}=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{\leq p^{a}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\right)
and

\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}}^{(a)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{a}-1}^{(a)}=\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{a}}
, we obtain
an exact sequence

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}_{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{+}\xrightarrow{\delta}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{a}}\left(=F_{X/S}^{(a)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(a)})\right)\rightarrow 0,

(2.8)

where the first arrow is the morphism obtained from the natural morphism
\mathcal{D}^{(a-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(a)}
.
Let us take a local section
\partial
of
\mathcal{T}^{(a)}
.
There exists locally a section
\widetilde{\partial}
in
\delta^{-1}((F_{X/S}^{(a)})^{-1}(\partial))\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{\leq p^{a}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{(a)}_{+}\right)
.
The exactness of (
2.8
) and the definition of
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
imply that
the
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear endomorphism
\nabla_{\partial}^{[a]}:=\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow{(a)}}(\widetilde{\partial})
of
\mathcal{F}^{[a]}
does not depend on the choice of
\widetilde{\partial}
(i.e., depends only on
\partial
).
Hence, we obtain a well-defined morphism

\displaystyle\nabla^{[a]}\left(\text{or}\ (\nabla^{(m)})^{[a]}\right):\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]}

(2.9)

determined by assigning
\partial\mapsto\nabla_{\partial}^{[a]}
.
This morphism satisfies the Leibniz rule and hence defines
an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection.
We thus obtain a flat module

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})

(2.10)

on the log curve
X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The collection
\{\mathcal{F}^{[a]}\}_{0\leq a\leq m+1}
forms a decreasing filtration on
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})=\mathcal{F}^{[a+1]}
for every
a=0,1,\cdots,m
.

2.2.
The relative
p
-Hitchin base

We use the notation
\mathfrak{c}
to denote the GIT quotient
\mathfrak{g}/\!/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
of
\mathfrak{g}
by the adjoint
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action.
The
k
-scheme
\mathfrak{c}
admits a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action coming from the homotheties on
\mathfrak{g}
.
If
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on either
X
or
X^{(m+1)}
, then there exists a natural identification between the affine space associated to
\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes\ell}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}
, where
\mathcal{L}^{\times}
denotes the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{L}
.
That is to say, there exists a canonical identification

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}\cong\mathbb{V}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes\ell}).

(2.11)

Denote by

\displaystyle B_{\mathscr{X}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}\right)

(2.12)

the set-valued
contravariant functor on
\mathcal{S}ch_{/S}
which assigns, to any
S
-scheme
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
,
the set of morphisms
S^{\prime}\times_{S}X\rightarrow(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}
over
X

(resp.,
S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{(m+1)}\rightarrow(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}
over
X^{(m+1)}
).
This functor
can be represented by a smooth
S
-scheme.
Indeed, the identification (
2.11
) yields an isomorphism of
S
-schemes

\displaystyle B_{\mathscr{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}\cdot\ell}))\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{V}(f_{*}^{(m+1)}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}))\right),

(2.13)

which is functorial with respect to
S
.
In particular, the relative dimension of
B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}
is computed by the following chain of equalities:

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}(B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}/S)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rk}\left(f_{*}^{(m+1)}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})\right)

(2.14)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=2}^{n}\mathrm{rk}\left(f_{*}^{(m+1)}((\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=2}^{n}\left(\ell\cdot(2g-2+r)+1-g\right)

(2.15)

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r.

Since
F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(\Omega^{(m+1)})\cong\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}}
, the pull-back via
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
gives rise to
an
S
-morphism

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathscr{X}}:B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}\rightarrow B_{\mathscr{X}},

(2.16)

and it makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{B_{\mathscr{X}}^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.13
)
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\mathscr{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}(f_{*}^{(m+1)}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{\mathscr{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
2.13
)
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}\cdot\ell})),}

where the right-hand vertical arrow arises from the direct sum
\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}\omega_{\ell}
of the morphisms

\displaystyle\omega_{\ell}:(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}\rightarrow\left(F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}((\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}))\cong\right)F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}\cdot\ell})

(2.21)

induced by
the adjunction relations “
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(-)\dashv F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(-)
”.

Proposition 2.1
.

(i)

For each
\ell=2,\cdots,n
,
the direct image

\displaystyle f^{(m+1)}_{*}(\omega_{\ell}):f^{(m+1)}_{*}((\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})\rightarrow f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}\cdot\ell})

(2.22)

of
\omega_{\ell}
via
f^{(m+1)}

is injective, and its cokernel is flat over
S
.

(ii)

The morphism
\iota_{\mathscr{X}}
is a closed immersion.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from an argument similar to the proof of
[
Wak4
, Proposition 3.23]
.
Assertion (ii) follows from assertion (i) together with the commutativity of (
2.2
), since the right-hand vertical arrow is obtained by applying the functor
\mathcal{S}pec(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(-)^{\vee})
to the morphism
\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}f^{(m+1)}_{*}(\omega_{\ell})
.
∎

Next, we shall write

\displaystyle B_{\mathscr{X},0}^{\flat}

(2.23)

for the subfunctor of
B_{\mathscr{X}}^{\flat}
that classifies, for each
S
-scheme
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
, morphisms
S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{(m+1)}\rightarrow(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}

inducing the zero section of
\mathfrak{c}
when restricted to every marked point
\sigma_{i}^{(m+1)}
of
\mathscr{X}^{(m+1)}
under the identification
\sigma^{(m+1)*}_{i}((\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\times})\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}\times_{k}S
arising from the residue isomorphism.
This functor can be represented by a closed subscheme of
B_{\mathscr{X}}^{\flat}
, and (the resp’d portion of) the isomorphism (
2.13
) restricts to
an isomorphism

\displaystyle B_{\mathscr{X},0}^{\flat}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{V}(f^{(m+1)}_{*}(\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}(-D^{(m+1)}))),

(2.24)

where
D^{(m+1)}
denotes the relative effective divisor on
X^{(m+1)}
defined as the union of
\sigma^{(m+1)}_{i}
’s (
i=1,\cdots,r
).
Similarly to (
2.14
),
the relative dimension of
B_{\mathscr{X},0}^{\flat}/S
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}(B_{\mathscr{X},0}^{\flat}/S)=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r.

(2.25)

2.3.
p^{m+1}
-curvature of a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module

Note that the image of the natural morphism
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m+1)}
coincides with
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m+1)}
.
If
\varpi:\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{p^{m+1}-1}^{(m+1)}
denotes the resulting surjection, then the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\varpi}\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m+1)}

(2.26)

is an isomorphism.
The composite
\varpi^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}
of
\varpi
and the inverse of (
2.26
) defines a split surjection of the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\rightarrow\left(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}-1}^{(m)}=\right)\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\rightarrow 0.

(2.27)

Thus, we obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism

\displaystyle\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}:\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{m+1}}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)},

(2.28)

where the first arrow denotes the split injection of (
2.27
) corresponding to
\varpi^{\prime}
.
This morphism coincides, via the adjunction relation
F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(-)\dashv F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(-)
, with the
p^{m+1}
-curvature map
\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow F_{X/S*}^{(m+1)}(\mathcal{D}^{(m)})
discussed in
[
Ohk
, Definition 3.10]
.

Now, let us consider a rank
n
vector bundle

\mathcal{F}
on
X
equipped with
a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
.
Recall that the composite

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla):\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\xrightarrow{\psi_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}}\mathcal{D}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})

(2.29)

is called the
p^{m+1}
-curvature (map)
of
\nabla
.
As proved in
[
Ohk
, Lemma 4.15]
, the image of
\psi(\nabla)
actually lies in
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}):=\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{F})\left(\subseteq\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})\right)
.
Also, if
a
is a nonnegative integer with

a<m
, then we see that
the
p^{a+1}
-curvature of
\nabla^{(m)\Rightarrow(a)}
vanishes.

We denote by

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}:=[(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{g})/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}]

(2.30)

the quotient stack of
(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{g}
by the adjoint
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action.
Then,
the pair
(\mathcal{F},\psi(\nabla))
specifies, via the quotient
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_{n})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
, a global section of this stack over
X
.
The composite of this section with the natural quotient

\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow(\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}})^{\times}\times^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathfrak{c}

defines an
S
-rational point

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}(\nabla)\in B_{\mathscr{X}}(S)

(2.31)

of

B_{\mathscr{X}}
.
Under the identification (
2.13
),
this
S
-rational point corresponds to the coefficients
a_{\ell}\in H^{0}(X,\Omega^{\otimes p^{m+1}\cdot\ell})
(
2\leq\ell\leq n
)
of
the characteristic polynomial

\displaystyle\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla)):=\mathrm{det}(t-\breve{\psi}(\nabla))=t^{n}+a_{2}\cdot t^{n-2}+a_{3}\cdot t^{n-3}+\cdots+a_{n},

(2.32)

where
\breve{\psi}(\nabla)
denotes the trace-free part of the
p^{m+1}
-curvature
\psi(\nabla)
, i.e.,

\breve{\psi}(\nabla):=\psi(\nabla)-\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathrm{tr}(\psi(\nabla))\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let us keep the above notation, and we regard
B_{\mathscr{X}}^{\flat}(S)
as a subset of
B_{\mathscr{X}}(S)
via
\iota_{\mathscr{X}}
.
Then, the
S
-rational point

\overline{\psi}(\nabla)
lies in
B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}(S)
, or equivalently, the element
a_{\ell}
lies in the subspace
H^{0}(X^{(m+1)},(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})
via the injection (
2.22
) for every
\ell=2,\cdots,n
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})
)
the subsheaf of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}^{[m]})
)
consisting of endomorphisms preserving the
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
(resp., the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
\nabla^{[m]}
).
The sheaf
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})
)
carries a natural
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module structure.
The
p^{m+1}
-curvature
\psi(\nabla):\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F})
of
\nabla

(resp.,
p
-curvature
\psi(\nabla^{[m]}):(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})^{\otimes p}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{F}^{[m]})
of
\nabla^{[m]}
)
restricts to an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-linear morphism

\displaystyle\psi(\nabla)^{\flat}:\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla)\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \psi(\nabla^{[m]})^{\flat}:\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]})\right)

(2.33)

under the identification
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\cong\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{m+1}}
(resp.,
F^{*}_{X^{(m)}/S}(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})\cong(\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)})^{\otimes p}
).
By the definition of this morphism, the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}^{(m+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi(\nabla)^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\psi(\nabla^{[m]})^{\flat}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}^{[m]},\nabla^{[m]}),}

where the lower horizontal arrow
\mathrm{res}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
is given by
h\mapsto h|_{\mathcal{F}^{[m]}}

for any
h\in\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
.

We here denote by
U
the smooth locus of
X\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
relative to
S
, which is
a scheme-theoretic dense open subscheme of
X
.
Then,
\mathrm{res}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism over
U^{(m+1)}
.
According to
[
ChZh
, Proposition 3.1]
(or
[
LasPa1
, Proposition 3.2]
,
[
Wak4
, Proposition-Definition 3.24]
),
the characteristic polynomial
\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla^{[m]})|_{U})
of the traceless
p
-curvature
\breve{\psi}(\nabla^{[m]})|_{U}
lies in
\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}H^{0}(U^{(m+1)},(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})
.
The commutativity of (
2.3
) implies
that this characteristic polynomial
coincides with

\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla)|_{U})
via
\mathrm{res}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
,
and hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla)|_{U})\in\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}H^{0}(U^{(m+1)},(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell}).

(2.38)

Furthermore, by an argument similar to the proof of
[
Wak4
, Proposition 3.3]
,
the cokernel of
\omega_{\ell}
for every
\ell
is relatively torsion-free over
S
.
It follows from this fact together with (
2.38
) that

\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla)
lies in
\bigoplus_{\ell=2}^{n}H^{0}(X^{(m+1)},(\Omega^{(m+1)})^{\otimes\ell})
.
This completes the proof of this assertion.
∎

2.4.
Exponents of
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-modules

In what follows, let us consider the situation where the underlying curve is a relative formal disk over
S
.
We shall set
U_{\oslash}:=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!])
, where
t
denotes a formal parameter.
For simplicity, we write
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}:=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\oslash}}
.
We equip
U_{\oslash}
with the log structure associated to
the marked point

\sigma_{\oslash}:S\rightarrow U_{\oslash}

corresponding to the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{S}[\![t]\!]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}
given by
t\mapsto 0
.
More explicitly, this log structure is given by
the monoid morphism
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
given by
\ell\mapsto t^{\ell}
.
Let
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
denote the resulting log scheme.

We then define a sheaf of noncommutative rings on
U_{\oslash}
by

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}_{\oslash}:=\varprojlim_{\ell\geq 1}\mathcal{D}_{U_{\oslash,\ell}^{\mathrm{log}}/S}^{(m)}
,
where
U_{\oslash,\ell}^{\mathrm{log}}
(
\ell\geq 0
) denotes the strict closed subscheme of
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
determined by the ideal sheaf
(t^{\ell})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
.
This sheaf carries two
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-module structures
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
and
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
arising from those of
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{U^{\mathrm{log}}_{\oslash,n}/k}^{(m)}
’s and
{{}^{R}}\mathcal{D}_{U^{\mathrm{log}}_{\oslash,n}/k}^{(m)}
’s (defined as in Section
2.1
), respectively.
The
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
-module
{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
admits a decomposition as a direct sum
\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\cdot\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
, where
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}
’s are the sections associated to the logarithmic coordinate
x
, as in
[
Wak14
, Section 2.2]
.

For each
d\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
,
we denote by
\widetilde{d}
the integer defined as the unique lifting of
d
via the natural surjection
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
0\leq\widetilde{d}<p^{m+1}
.
There exists a unique
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure

\displaystyle\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash})

(2.39)

on
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}
determined by the condition that
\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}(\partial^{\langle j\rangle})(t^{\ell})=q_{j}!\cdot\binom{\ell-\widetilde{d}}{j}\cdot t^{\ell}
for every
j,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
The resulting
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(m)}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})

(2.40)

is isomorphic to the unique extension of
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,0}^{(m)}
to
t^{-\widetilde{d}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\left(\supseteq\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\right)
.

The following lemma will be applied in the discussion of Section
3.6
several times.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
d
be an element of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{m+1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Then, the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(m)})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\twoheadrightarrow\sigma_{\oslash*}(\sigma^{*}_{\oslash}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}))

(2.41)

is surjective if and only if
d=0
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 4.4]
.
∎

Now, let us return to the global setting (hence
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{D}_{X^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}^{(m)}
for
\mathscr{X}:=(X/S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i})
as before).
Suppose that
S
is connected, and
let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a

\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module with vanishing
p^{m+1}
-curvature such that
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle of rank
n
.
Also, let
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
.
According to
[
Wak14
, Proposition-Definition 4.8]
, there exists
a unique multiset

\displaystyle e_{i}(\nabla):=[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]

(2.42)

with cardinality
n

satisfying the following condition:
for each
k
-rational point
q
of
S
,
there exists, after base-change along
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{S,q})\rightarrow S
, an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-modules
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}^{(m)}_{\oslash,d_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{F},\nabla)|_{U_{\oslash}}
under
any identification of
U_{\oslash}^{\mathrm{log}}
with the formal neighborhood of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})\left(\subseteq X\right)
.
We refer to
e_{i}(\nabla)
as the
(local) exponent
of
\nabla
at
\sigma_{i}
.

2.5.
Monodromy operators

We recall the notion of monodromy operator introduced in
[
Wak14
, Section 4.2]
.
Let us set

\mathcal{B}_{S}:=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}
,
where
\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle i\rangle}
’s are abstract symbols.
We equip
\mathcal{B}_{S}
with a structure of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra by declaring

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime}\rangle}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\sum_{j=\mathrm{max}\{j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\}}^{j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}}\frac{j!}{(j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}-j)!\cdot(j-j^{\prime})!\cdot(j-j^{\prime\prime})!}\cdot\frac{q_{j^{\prime}}!\cdot q_{j^{\prime\prime}}!}{q_{j}!}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}.

(2.43)

In particular,
\mathcal{B}_{S}
is a commutative
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra generated by the sections
\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle 1\rangle},\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p\rangle},\cdots,\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p^{m}\rangle}
.

Suppose that we are given
an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\mathcal{G}
equipped with
an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra morphism

\mu:\mathcal{B}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})
.
For each
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
we set

\mu^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}:=\mu(\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle p^{a}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})
.
The morphism
\mu
is completely determined by
the
(m+1)
-tuple

\displaystyle\mu^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\mu^{\langle 1\rangle},\mu^{\langle p\rangle}\cdots,\mu^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{G})^{\oplus(m+1)}.

(2.44)

Let
U_{\oslash}
,
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
, and
\sigma_{\oslash}
be as before.
It follows from (
2.3
) and (
2.43
) that the assignment
\partial^{\langle j\rangle}\mapsto\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}
(
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) determines an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebras

\displaystyle\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{B}_{S}.

(2.45)

Now, let us take a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
.
The
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
induces a
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})
-action
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\nabla)
on
\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})
.
This yields a composite

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla^{(m)}):\mathcal{B}_{S}\xrightarrow{\eqref{Eq48}^{-1}}\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(m)})\xrightarrow{\sigma^{*}_{\oslash}(\nabla)}\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})).

(2.46)

In particular, we obtain a tuple

\displaystyle\mu(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\mu(\nabla)^{\langle 1\rangle},\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p\rangle}\cdots,\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\oplus(m+1)}.

(2.47)

Since
\mathcal{B}_{S}
is commutative,
these endomorphisms
commute with one another.
We refer to
\mu(\nabla)
(and the associated tuple
\mu(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
) as
the
monodromy operator
of
\nabla
, following the terminology of
[
Wak14
, Definition 4.2]
.
Moreover,
for each
a=0,\cdots,m
,
we call
\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}
the
a
-th monodromy operator
of
\nabla
.
The relationship between the notions of exponent and monodromy operator is described in
[
Wak14
, Proposition-Definition 4.8]
.

Next, we shall write
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{0}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))
for the subspace of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))
consisting of endomorphisms with vanishing trace.
We shall
write

\displaystyle\breve{\mu}(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=(\breve{\mu}(\nabla)^{\langle 1\rangle},\breve{\mu}(\nabla)^{\langle p\rangle},\cdots,\breve{\mu}(\nabla)^{\langle p^{m}\rangle})\in(\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}^{0}(\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F})))^{\oplus(m+1)},

(2.48)

where

\breve{\mu}(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}:=\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}-\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathrm{tr}(\mu(\nabla)^{\langle p^{a}\rangle})\cdot\mathrm{id}_{\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F})}
.

Next,
let us return to the global setting.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be as before and fix
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
.
Also,
let us take a
\mathcal{D}^{(m)}
-module
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
.
Assume that
the image
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
is determined by “
t=0
” for some function
t
on its open neighborhood.
This assumption allows us to identify
U_{\oslash}
with the formal neighborhood of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
.
In particular, we obtain
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-module
(\mathcal{F}|_{U_{\oslash}},\nabla|_{U_{\oslash}})
, which determines
elements

\displaystyle\mu_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=\mu(\nabla|_{U_{\oslash}})^{\langle\bullet\rangle},\hskip 28.45274pt\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla)^{\langle\bullet\rangle}:=\breve{\mu}(\nabla|_{U_{\oslash}})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}

(2.49)

of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\oplus(m+1)}

under the natural identification
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F})=\sigma_{\oslash}^{*}(\mathcal{F}|_{U_{\oslash}})
.

3.
The moduli space of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

This section is devoted to the study of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers and their moduli space; for a detailed treatment of the subject, we refer the reader to
[
Wak14
]
.
We also introduce a generalization of the Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism, defined via higher-level
p
-curvature.
As an application, we establish several geometric properties of this morphism (cf. Theorems
3.21
,
3.23
).

3.1.
Dormant/
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

Let
(g,r)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
2g-2+r>0
,

S
a scheme (or more generally, a Deligne-Mumford stack) over
k
, and

\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
, as in the previous section.
Also, we set
N:=m+1
, i.e.,
N
is a positive integer.

Consider a pair
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
consisting of
a
B
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{B}
on
X
and an
(N-1)
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
.
(The precise definition of a higher-level PD stratification on a
G
-bundle will not be recalled here, as
the discussion below will only concern its translation into a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-actions on a vector bundle.
For full details, see
[
Wak14
, Definition 2.3]
.)
Denote by
\nabla_{\phi}
the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
corresponding to the
0
-PD stratification induced by
\phi
.

Definition 3.1

(cf.
[
Wak14
]
, Definitions 5.2, 5.3)
.

(i)

We say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
(or a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper of level
N
)
on
\mathscr{X}
if
the pair
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\nabla_{\phi})
forms a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper (equivalently, an
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{n}
-oper) on
\mathscr{X}
, in the sense of
[
Wak4
, Definition 2.1]
.
One can define the notion of an isomorphism between two
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.

(ii)

A
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
is said to be
dormant
if
the
p^{N}
-curvature of
\phi
(in the sense of
[
Wak14
, Definition 2.10, (ii)]
) vanishes identically.

To describe

\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers in terms of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules, we recall the notion of an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic introduced in
[
Wak14
]
, as follows.

Definition 3.2

(cf.
[
Wak14
]
, Definition 5.12)
.

An
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
of
\mathscr{X}
is a pair

\displaystyle\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})

(3.1)

consisting of a line bundle
\varTheta
on
X
and a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\vartheta}
on the line bundle
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}
.
We say that an
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
is
dormant
if

\nabla_{\vartheta}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

Let us take a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
\mathscr{X}
.
(According to
[
Wak14
, Proposition 5.14]
, such an object always exists.)
We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq n-1}\otimes\varTheta,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq n-j-1}\otimes\varTheta\ (j=0,\cdots,n).

(3.2)

Note that these vector bundles do not depend on the level
N
because of the assumption
n<p
.
Since
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}/\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j+1}
(
j=0,\cdots,n-1
)
can be identified with
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(n-j-1)}\otimes\varTheta
, we obtain a composite isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigotimes_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}/\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j+1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigotimes_{j=0}^{n-1}(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(n-j-1)}\otimes\varTheta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\otimes\varTheta^{\otimes n}.

(3.3)

Definition 3.3

(cf.
[
Wak14
]
, Definition 5.15)
.

(i)

A
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
on
\mathscr{X}
is a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
such that, for every
j=0,\cdots,n-1
,
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
induced by
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
restricts to an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\leq n-j-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{n-1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}.

(3.4)

We say that a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
is
dormant
if its
p^{N}
-curvature vanishes identically.

(ii)

Let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ}
and
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}
be (dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers on
\mathscr{X}
.
An
isomorphism of (dormant)
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers
from
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ}
to
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet}

is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules
(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\circ})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\bullet})
preserving the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}
.

Let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
be a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}
the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle obtained by projectivizing
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
.
Then,
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
induces an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}
on
\mathcal{E}
.
The filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{n}

defines a
B
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{B}
of
\mathcal{E}
, and
the pair

\displaystyle\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi_{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}})

(3.5)

constitutes a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper, since
the morphisms (
3.4
) associated to the various
j
’s are isomorphisms.
The resulting assignment
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
determines a well-defined bijection of sets

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{$(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{matrix}\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{$\mathrm{PGL}_{n}$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{matrix}\right)

(3.6)

(cf.
[
Wak14
, Theorem 5.18]
).

Moreover, since
\nabla_{\vartheta}
is dormant, we see that
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
is dormant if and only if the corresponding
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
is dormant.
It follows that
(
3.6
) restricts to a bijection

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{dormant $(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{matrix}\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{dormant $\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers on $\mathscr{X}$}\end{matrix}\right).

(3.7)

Definition 3.4
.

(i)

A
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
is said to be
p^{N}
-nilpotent
if the equality
\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}))=0
holds (cf. (
2.32
)).

(ii)

For
a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
,
we shall set

\displaystyle\psi\text{-}\mathrm{Char}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}):=\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})),

(3.8)

where
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
denotes
the corresponding
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper via (
3.6
).
(Note that
this definition
does not depend on the choice of a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta
.)
We say that
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
p^{N}
-nilpotent
if

\psi\text{-}\mathrm{Char}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})=0
, i.e.,
the corresponding
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
is
p^{N}
-nilpotent.

3.2.
Radii of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

Let us consider the direct product
\mathfrak{g}^{N}:=\mathfrak{g}\times\cdots\times\mathfrak{g}
of
N
copies of
\mathfrak{g}
.
The adjoint
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action on
\mathfrak{g}
induces a diagonal
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action on this product.
Accordingly, we define its GIT quotient by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{N}:=\mathfrak{g}^{N}/\!/\mathrm{PGL}_{n},

(3.9)

which is a
k
-scheme and coincides with
\mathfrak{c}
when
N=1
.
If
[\mathfrak{g}^{N}/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}]
denotes the quotient stack of
\mathfrak{g}^{N}
by the same
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action,
then there exists a natural morphism
[\mathfrak{g}^{N}/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}]\rightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N}
.

Given a positive integer
N^{\prime}
with
N^{\prime}\leq N
and an
r
-tuple
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S)^{r}
,
we set

\displaystyle\rho^{(N^{\prime})}:=(\rho_{i}^{(N^{\prime})})_{i=1}^{r},

(3.10)

where
\rho_{i}^{(N^{\prime})}
(
i=1,\cdots,r
) denotes the image of
\rho_{i}
via
\mathfrak{c}_{N}\rightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N^{\prime}}
induced from the projection onto the first
N^{\prime}
factors
\mathfrak{g}^{N}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{N^{\prime}}
.

Now, let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
be a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
For each
i=1,\cdots,r
,
the element
\mu_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
determines an
S
-rational point of
[\mathfrak{g}^{N}/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}]
via
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_{n})\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
.
The image of this point under the morphism
[\mathfrak{g}^{N}/\mathrm{PGL}_{n}]\rightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N}
defines an
S
-rational point

\displaystyle\rho_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S).

(3.11)

Moreover, given a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
,
we shall set

\displaystyle\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}):=\rho_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}})\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S),

(3.12)

where
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
is the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper corresponding to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
Note that
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
does not depend on the choice of
\vartheta
.

Definition 3.5
.

(i)

We refer to
\rho_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
(resp.,
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
)
as the
radius
of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
).

(ii)

Let

\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an element of
\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S)^{r}
.
We say that a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
(resp., a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
) is
of radii
\rho
if
\rho_{i}=\rho_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
(resp.,
\rho_{i}=\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
) for every
i=1,\cdots,r
.
When
r=0
, any
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper (resp.,
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper) is said to be
of radii
\emptyset
.

For an element
d
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
,
let
\widetilde{d}_{[0]},\cdots,\widetilde{d}_{[N-1]}
be the collection of integers uniquely determined by the condition that

\widetilde{d}=\sum_{a=0}^{N-1}p^{a}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{[a]}
and
0\leq\widetilde{d}_{[a]}<p
(
a=0,\cdots,N-1
).
Given each
a=0,\cdots,N-1
, we write
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}:=\sum_{b=0}^{a}p^{b}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{[b]}
, i.e., the remainder obtained by dividing
\widetilde{d}
by
p^{a+1}
, and write

d_{[a]}
(resp.,
d_{[0,a]}
) for the image of
\widetilde{d}_{[a]}
(resp.,
\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}
)
via the natural projection
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{p}

(resp.,
\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/p^{a+1}\mathbb{Z}
).

Denote by
\Delta
the image of the diagonal embedding
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
, which is a group homomorphism.
In particular, this gives rise the quotient set
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
.
The set
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
is equipped with the action of the symmetric group of
n
letters
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
by permutation;
this induces a well-defined
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-action on
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
.
Hence, we obtain the quotient sets

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta,

(3.13)

and there is a natural projection map

\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
.
Each element of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
can be interpreted as a multiset of elements of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
with cardinality
n
.

Let
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,N}
denote the subset of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
consisting of multisets
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
such that
the induced elements
d_{1[0]},\cdots,d_{n[0]}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}

are pairwise distinct.
(In particular,
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,N}
can be identified with the set of
n
-element subsets of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
whose images in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
are distinct.)
Then, we obtain the set

\displaystyle\Xi_{n,N}\ \left(\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta\right)

(3.14)

consisting of all elements admitting representatives in
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n,N}
.
We occasionally regard
\Xi_{n,N}
as the scheme defined to be the disjoint union of copies of
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
indexed by the elements in
\Xi_{n,N}
.

Example 3.6
.

Let us consider the case
n=2
.
Denote by

(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
the set of equivalence classes of elements
a\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
, in which
a
and
-a
are identified.
Then, the assignment

[a,b]\mapsto\overline{\big(\frac{a-b}{2}\big)}

determines a bijection

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{2}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{2}/\Delta\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}.

(3.15)

Under
this bijection,

\Xi_{2,N}
corresponds to the subset
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
of
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
, i.e., the image of

(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
under the natural projection
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
.

Let
\vec{d}:=(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})
be an element of
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
.
For each
j=0,\cdots,N-1
, denote by
D(\vec{d})_{[j]}
the diagonal
n\times n
matrix
defined as

\displaystyle D(\vec{d})_{[j]}:=\begin{pmatrix}(-d_{1})_{[j]}&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&(-d_{2})_{[j]}&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&(-d_{n-1})_{[j]}&0\\
0&0&\cdots&0&(-d_{n})_{[j]}\end{pmatrix}

(3.16)

Also, denote by
\overline{D}(\vec{d})

the element of
\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S)
defined to be the image of
(D(\vec{d})_{[0]},\cdots,D(\vec{d})_{[N-1]})
via the quotient
\mathfrak{g}^{N}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N}
.
The map
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}\rightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N}
given by
\vec{d}\mapsto\overline{D}(\vec{d})
factors through
the quotient
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
.
The resulting morphism

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta\rightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S)

(3.17)

is injective.
Using this injection, we may regard
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
(and hence
\Xi_{n,N}
) as a subset of
\mathfrak{c}_{N}(S)
.

The following assertion for dormant opers was already established in
[
Wak14
, Proposition 6.14]
.
Indeed, the exponent of a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(\\
N)},\vartheta)
-oper at each marked point is mapped to its radius via
\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}/\Delta
.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
) be a
p^{N}
-nilpotent
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper (resp., a
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper) on
\mathscr{X}
with
N>1
.
Then, the radius of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
(resp.,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
) at
\sigma_{i}
lies in
\Xi_{n,N}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the non-resp’d assertion because of the definition of
p^{N}
-nilpotency for
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers.
To this end,
we may assume, without loss of generality, that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
for a local
k
-algebra
(R,\mathfrak{m})
with
R/\mathfrak{m}=k
.

Denote by

\vartheta^{\prime}
the
n^{(N-1)}
-theta characteristic obtained from
\vartheta
via level reduction, and by

\nabla^{\prime}
the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N-1)},\vartheta^{\prime})
-oper obtained from
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.
Since
\nabla^{\prime}
is dormant,
we may associate to it the exponent
[d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}]
at
\sigma_{i}
, where
d_{1},\cdots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N-1}\mathbb{Z}
.
Fix a suitable trivialization
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus n}
, under which we may arrange that

\displaystyle\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}\left(=\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\prime})^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}(-d_{1})_{[a]}&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&(-d_{2})_{[a]}&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&(-d_{n-1})_{[a]}&0\\
0&0&\cdots&0&(-d_{n})_{[a]}\end{pmatrix}

(3.18)

for each
a=0,\cdots,N-2
.
Moreover, the dormancy condition on
\nabla^{\prime}
implies that
the diagonal entries
(-d_{1})_{[0]},\cdots,(-d_{n})_{[0]}
of

\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle 1\rangle}
are pairwise distinct (cf.
[
Wak14
, Proposition 6.3, (ii)]
).
As
\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{N-1}\rangle}
commutes with
\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle 1\rangle}
,
the matrix
\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{N-1}\rangle}
must be diagonal, i.e., expressed as

\displaystyle\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{N-1}\rangle}=\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&h_{2}&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&h_{n-1}&0\\
0&0&\cdots&0&h_{n}\end{pmatrix}

(3.19)

for some
h_{1},\cdots,h_{n}\in H^{0}(S,\mathcal{O}_{S})
.

The assumption that
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
is
p^{N}
-nilpotent implies

\displaystyle(\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{N-1}\rangle})^{p}-\breve{\mu}_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle p^{N-1}\rangle}=0.

(3.20)

It follows that
h_{j}^{p}-h_{j}=0
for every
j=1,\cdots,n
, i.e., the elements
h_{1},\cdots,h_{n}
lie in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Therefore, for each
j=1,\cdots,n
, there exists a lifting
d^{+}_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
of
d_{j}
satisfying

(-d^{+}_{j})_{[N-1]}=h_{j}
.
The multiset
[d^{+}_{1},\cdots,d^{+}_{n}]
belongs to
\Xi_{n,N}
, which coincides with the radius of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
at
\sigma_{i}
.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

3.3.
The moduli spaces of dormant/
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
denote
the moduli stack classifying
r
-pointed stable curves of genus
g
over
k
.
Also, let us fix an
r
-tuple
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(k)^{r}
, where we set
\rho:=\emptyset
if
r=0
.
We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r};\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}};\text{resp.,}\ \mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\right)

(3.21)

to be the category that classifies pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
, where
\mathscr{X}
denotes a pointed curve
\mathscr{X}
in
\mathrm{ob}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r})
and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
denotes a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper (resp., a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho
; resp., a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho
; resp., a
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper of radii
\rho
) on
\mathscr{X}
.

Forgetting the data of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers yields a projection

\displaystyle\Pi_{g,r}:\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\ \Big(\text{resp.,}\ \Pi_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r};\hskip 8.53581pt

(3.22)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}\ \Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r};\text{resp.,}\ \Pi^{\prime\prime}_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\Big).

Since any
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper does not have nontrivial automorphisms (cf.
[
Wak14
, Proposition 5.5]
),
the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}
; resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
; resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
) is fibered in equivalence relations over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(in the sense of
[
FGA
, Definition 3.39]
), i.e.,
specifies a set-valued étale sheaf on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.

The following assertion was previously
established in
[
Moc2
]
for
(n,N)=(2,1)
,
[
Wak4
]
for general
n
with
N=1
, and
[
Wak14
]
for arbitrary
(n,N)
.

Theorem 3.8
.

(i)

The category
\mathcal{O}p_{N,g,r}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}
) can be represented by
a nonempty (resp., a possibly empty)
Deligne-Mumford stacks of finite type over
k
, and the projection
\Pi_{g,r}
(resp.,

\Pi_{\rho,g,r}
) is represented by schemes and is affine.
Moreover, when
N=1
, the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}
is nonempty.

(ii)

The category
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
can be represented by a (possibly empty) proper Deligne-Mumford stack over
k
, and the projection
\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g,r}
is finite.
Moreover,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is empty unless
\rho
lies in
\Xi_{n,N}^{r}
.

(iii)

Suppose that
n=2
and
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
.
Then,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is smooth over
k
and of dimension
3g-3+r
.
Moreover,
\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g,r}
is
faithfully flat and generically étale.

Proof.

The first assertion of (i) follows from
[
Wak14
, Theorem 5.22, Corollary 6.28, (i)]
.
The second assertion follows from
[
Wak4
, Theorem A]
.
Assertion (ii) follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 6.14, Theorem 6.17]
.
Also, assertion (iii) follows from
[
Wak14
, Corollary 8.17, Theorem 8.29]
.
∎

Regarding the moduli stack of
p^{N}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-opers,
we obtain the following assertion, which follows immediately from
the definition of
p^{N}
-nilpotency.

Proposition 3.9
.

The category
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
can be represented by a (possibly empty) closed substack of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}
, and the inclusion
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is a closed immersion.

We remark that the nonemptiness of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(as well as
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
) depends on the choice of
\rho
.
In the case
n=2
,
a combinatorial analysis of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
was carried out in

[
Wak14
]
(or
[
Moc2
]
for
N=1
),
which provides a criterion for when this stack is nonempty.
Applying this result, we obtain the following assertion, which will be applied in the proof of Proposition
3.14
.

Proposition 3.10
.

Suppose that
n=2
, and
let
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an element of

\Xi_{2,N}^{r}

with
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
.
Then, there exists an
r
-tuple
\widetilde{\rho}\in\mathfrak{c}_{N+1}(k)^{r}
with
\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}=\rho
(cf. (
3.10
)) and
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

Let us take a totally degenerate
r
-pointed stable curve
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
of genus
g
over
k
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Definition 6.25]
for the definition of a totally degenerate curve).
Since
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is nonempty,
the various properties of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
recalled in Theorem
3.8
, together with the irreducibility of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
, imply that the projection
\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g,r}:\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
is surjective.
The fiber of this projection over the point classifying
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
is nonempty, so
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
of radii
\rho
.

Next, let
G
denote the dual semi-graph of
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
, which is a connected trivalent semi-graph of type
(g,r)
, in the sense of
[
Wak14
, Definition 6.6, (ii) and (iii)]
.
For a positive integer
N_{0}
, recall from
[
Wak14
, Definition 10.17]

the notion of a
balance
(p,N_{0})
-edge numbering of prescribed radii
on
G
(where
G
is equipped with an ordering on the branches incident to each vertex).
Roughly speaking, such a numbering is a certain collection of nonnegative integers
(a_{e})_{e\in E}
indexed by the edge set
E
of
G
.
It follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 10.18]
that, for each
r
-tuple
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N_{0}}^{r}
,
there exists a bijective correspondence

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of dormant $\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N_{0})}$-opers}\\
\text{on $\mathscr{X}^{\prime}$ of radii $\rho_{0}$}\end{matrix}\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of}\\
\text{balanced $(p,N_{0})$-edge}\\
\text{numberings on $G$ of radii $\rho_{0}$}\end{matrix}\right).

(3.23)

In particular, the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
corresponds to a balanced
(p,N)
-edge numbering
(a_{e})_{e\in E}
on
G
of radii
\rho
.
If
e_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,r
) denotes the open edge corresponding to the
i
-th marked point
\sigma_{i}
,
then the element

\overline{\left(\frac{2a_{e_{i}}+1}{2}\right)}
in
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
coincides with
\rho_{i}
under the identification

\Xi_{2,N}=(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
given by (
3.15
).

Now, define
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}
to be the class
\overline{\left(\frac{2a_{e_{i}}+1}{2}\right)}
, regarded as an element of
\Xi_{2,N+1}\left(=(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N+1}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}\right)
.
The collection
\widetilde{\rho}:=(\widetilde{\rho}_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
satisfies

\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}=\rho
.
Then, the collection
(a_{e})_{e\in E}
can be regarded as a balanced
(p,N+1)
-edge numbering on
G
, and its radii are given by
\widetilde{\rho}
.
Applying the bijection (
3.23
) to the case
N_{0}=N+1
,
we obtain a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N+1)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
of radii
\widetilde{\rho}
corresponding to
(a_{e})_{e}
.
This shows that
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
,
completing the proof of this proposition.
∎

3.4.
The adjoint bundle of a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper

Let
\mathscr{X}
be as before and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)

a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow X
(the structure morphism of) the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-bundle induced from
\mathcal{E}_{B}
.
Since
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
is affine,
\mathcal{E}
corresponds to an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Pulling-back the log structure of
X^{\mathrm{log}}
along
\pi
yields
a log structure on
\mathcal{E}
, and we denote the resulting log scheme by
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}
.
The
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action on
\mathcal{E}
induces a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-action on the direct image
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})
of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
, so we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}:=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{n}},

(3.24)

i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}
is the subsheaf of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-invariant sections of
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}})
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.,
\mathfrak{b}_{\mathcal{E}_{B}}
) the adjoint vector bundle of
\mathcal{E}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{B}
), i.e., the vector bundle on
X
associated to
\mathcal{E}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{B}
) via change of structure group by the adjoint representation
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
(resp.,
B\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{b})
).
We regard
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule of
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})
under the natural identification
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/X})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}\left(=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/X^{\mathrm{log}}})^{\mathrm{PGL}_{2}}\right)
.
Differentiating
\pi
gives rise to a short exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}}\xrightarrow{d_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{T}\rightarrow 0

(3.25)

(cf.
[
Wak4
, Section 1.2.5]
).

Note that
\phi
induces a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})

(3.26)

on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
To be precise, if
\phi^{\natural\natural}
denotes the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\phi
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Remark 2.6]
), then
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}
is
given by
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}(D)(v)=[\phi^{\natural\natural}(D),v]
for any local sections
D\in\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
and
v\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
In particular, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
, which has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature.

The following assertion generalizes
[
Wak14
, Proposition 8.2]
to the higher-rank setting.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let us fix

a\in\{0,1,\cdots,N-1\}
.
Recall that
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
induces a flat module

(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]}\rightarrow\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]})
on
X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
with vanishing
p
-curvature
(cf. (
2.10
)).

(i)

The
following equalities of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules hold:

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}])=f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}))=\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}))=0.

(3.27)

(ii)

Denote by
D^{(a)}

the reduced effective divisor on
X^{(a)}
determined by the union of the marked points of
\mathscr{X}^{(a)}
.
Also, for each flat module
(\mathcal{V},\nabla_{\mathcal{V}})
on
X^{(a)\mathrm{log}}/S^{\mathrm{log}}
, we write
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{V}(-D^{(a)})
and write
{{}^{c}}\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}
for the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection on
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{V}\right)
obtained by restricting
\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}])=f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}))=\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}))=0.

(3.28)

(iii)

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}])

is a vector bundle on
S
of rank

(n^{2}-1)(2g-2+r)
.
Also,

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}))
and
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}_{\phi}))
) are
vector bundles on
S
of
rank

(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}-n)r

and

(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r
,
respectively.

Proof.

We shall prove assertions (i)-(iii) simultaneously by induction on
a
.
For simplicity, we write

\mathcal{F}^{[a]}:=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[a]}
and
\nabla^{[a]}:=\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[a]}
.

Let us begin with
the base step, i.e.,
the case
a=0
.
Assertions (i) and (iii) in this case
were already established in
[
Wak4
, Propositions 6.5, (ii), and 6.18]
.
To prove assertion (ii),
we recall from
[
Wak4
, Section 6.5.1]
that
the
1
-st monodromy operator

\displaystyle\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle}:\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]})\rightarrow\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]})

(3.29)

(for every
i=1,\cdots,r
) of
\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}
at
\sigma_{i}
can be identified with the adjoint operator
on
\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]})
determined by the monodromy
(in the sense of
[
Wak4
, Definition 1.46]
) of the
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection induced from
\phi
.
This endomorphism fits into the following short exact sequence of complexes (concentrated at degrees
0
and
1
) under the natural identification
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]})=\sigma^{*}_{i}(\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[0]})
arising from the residue isomorphism
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\Omega)\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
