\textstyle{{{}^{c}}\mathcal{F}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla^{[0]}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i*}(\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigoplus_{i}\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega\otimes{{}^{c}}\mathcal{F}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[0]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i*}(\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The equality
f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[0]}))=0
holds by assertion (i), so
this short exact sequence gives rise to
a long exact sequence of hypercohomology sheaves

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Ker}(\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}])

(3.34)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Coker}(\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow 0,

which is functorial with respect to
S
.
According to
[
Wak4
, Proposition 6.17]
,

\mathrm{Ker}(\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle})
and

\mathrm{Coker}(\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle})
are vector bundles on
S
of the same rank.

On the other hand,
since the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}
yields an isomorphism of flat modules
(\mathcal{F}^{[0]},\nabla^{[0]})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{F}^{[0]\vee},\nabla^{[0]\vee})
,
it follows from
[
Wak4
, Corollary 6.16]

that
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])

and
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}])
are vector bundles on
S
of the same rank.
Hence, the fourth arrow

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Coker}(\mu_{i}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\langle 1\rangle})

in the above exact sequence must be surjective, and the morphism

\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}])
induced by the inclusion
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[0]}]
turns out to be injective.
This fact together with assertion (i) in the base step shows
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])=0
.

Moreover, consider
the conjugate spectral sequence

{}^{\prime\prime}E_{2}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}}f_{*}(\mathcal{H}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}]))\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])

(3.35)

associated to
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}]
(cf.
[
Wak4
, Eq. (757)]
).
Since the equality
\mathbb{R}^{j}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}))=0
holds for every
j\geq 2
due to
\mathrm{dim}(X/S)=1
,
the morphism

\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}))

induced by (
3.35
) is surjective, which implies
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}))=0
.
The equality
f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}))=0
follows from
\left(f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[0]}))\subseteq\right)f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[0]}))=0
obtained above,
so the proof of the base step is completed.

Next, to carry out the induction step, we assume that assertions (i)-(iii) with
a
replaced by
a-1
(where
a\geq 1
) have been proved.
We begin by considering assertion (i).
Since
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})\subseteq\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]})
,
the equality

\displaystyle f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0

(3.36)

follows immediately from the induction hypothesis
f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))=0
.
Next, we consider the Hodge-to-de Rham spectral sequence

{}^{\prime}E_{1}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{q_{1}}[\nabla^{[a]}])\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

(3.37)

associated to the complex
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]
(cf.
[
Wak8
, Eq. (755)]
).
Since
\mathrm{dim}(X/S)=1
, the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})=0
holds, and
the sequence

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\nabla^{[a]})}\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\rightarrow 0

(3.38)

induced by
(
3.37
) is exact.
According to

[
Ogu2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
(or the discussion
following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
),

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))
.
By the induction hypothesis,
the equality
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))=0
holds, so the exactness of (
3.38
) implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])=0.

(3.39)

Moreover,
the conjugate spectral sequence

{}^{\prime\prime}E_{2}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}}f_{*}(\mathcal{H}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]))\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

(3.40)

associated to
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]
gives rise to
the surjection

\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))
.
Hence, it follows from (
3.39
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0.

(3.41)

By (
3.36
), (
3.39
), and (
3.41
),
the proof of assertion (i) is completed.

Assertion (ii) follows immediately from an argument similar to the corresponding assertion in the base step, using the assertion (i) just proved
and the conjugate spectral sequence
{}^{\prime\prime}E_{2}^{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}}f_{*}(\mathcal{H}^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}]))

\Rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}])
instead of (
3.35
).

Finally, we consider assertion (iii).
It follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 2.15, (i)]
that
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is a relatively torsion-free sheaf on
X^{(a+1)}
.
Also, since

\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is isomorphic to
\Omega^{(a+1)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})

(cf.
[
Ogu2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
or the comment following
[
Ogu1
, Proposition 1.2.4]
),

\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})
is a relatively torsion-free sheaf on
X^{(a+1)}
.
By the equality
\mathbb{R}^{2}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0
and (
3.41
),
both
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))
and
f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

turn out to be vector bundles (cf.
[
Har
, Chapter III, Theorem 12.11, (b)]
).
Moreover, the exactness of
the sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow f_{*}(\Omega^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\left(=f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])

(3.42)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{[a]})\left(=\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))\right)\rightarrow 0

induced by (
3.37
) and the induction hypothesis together imply that

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])
is a vector bundle.

In what follows, we compute the ranks of the vector bundles under consideration.
To this end, we may assume that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Fix a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
\mathscr{X}
.
The dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
corresponds to a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.
For each
i=1,\cdots,r
, let
[d_{i,1},\cdots,d_{i,n}]
be the exponent of
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
at the marked point
\sigma_{i}
.
After possibly reordering the elements in this multiset, we may assume that
0\leq\widetilde{d}_{i,1}<\widetilde{d}_{i,2}<\cdots<\widetilde{d}_{i,n}<p^{N}
.

By
[
Wak4
, Corollary 6.16]
in the case where “
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
” is taken to be
(\mathcal{F}^{[a]},\nabla^{[a]})
,
there exists a canonical isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

\displaystyle H^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{0}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))^{\vee}.

(3.43)

In particular,
we have

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))=h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]})).

(3.44)

Next,
the isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules

(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee},(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\vee})

induced by the Killing form on
\mathfrak{g}

yields an isomorphism of flat modules

({{}^{c}}\mathcal{F}^{[a]},{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\sim}({{}^{c}}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee})^{[a]},{{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})
.
This restricts to an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})
, which implies

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})).

(3.45)

Hence,
the following chain of equalities holds:

\displaystyle h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ddE1}}}{{=}}h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))

(3.46)

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))-R_{a}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{dE155}}}{{=}}h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))-R_{a}

\displaystyle=\left(h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+(n-1)r+R_{a}\right)-R_{a}

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+(n-1)r

(cf. (
3.50
) for the definition of
R_{a}
),
where the second and fourth equalities follow from Lemma
3.13
stated below.

On the other hand,
there exists a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow H^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}])\rightarrow H^{0}(X^{(a)},\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow 0

(3.47)

induced by (
3.40
).
This implies

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a]}))

(3.48)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}(\mathbb{H}^{1}(X^{(a)},\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{[a]}]))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))+h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{[a-1]}))

\displaystyle=\left((n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}-n)r\right)+\left((n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r\right)

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(2g-2+r),

where the second equality follows from the exactness of (
3.42
) and the third equality follows from the induction hypothesis.
Consequently, it follows from
(
3.46
) and (
3.48
) that

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}))

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}-n)r,

(3.49)

\displaystyle h^{0}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}))

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Corollary 3.12
.

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
(resp.,
f_{*}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
) is a vector bundle on
S
of rank
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}-n)r
(resp.,
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r
).

Proof.

By the definition of
\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]}
,
the equality

\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})=\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})

holds.
On the other hand, it follows from
[
Ogu2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
(or the discussion following
[
Ogu1
, Chapter I, Proposition 1.2.4]
) that the Cartier operator associated to
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})
induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})
.
These facts together imply

\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\cong\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]}))

and
f_{*}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\cong f_{*}(\mathrm{Coker}(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]}))
.
Hence, the assertion
follows from Proposition
3.11
, (ii).
∎

The following lemma was applied in the proof of Proposition
3.11
above.
See
[
Wak14
, Lemma 8.3]
for the case of
n=2
.

Lemma 3.13
.

We retain the notation in the proof of Proposition
3.11
.
Let
a\in\{1,\cdots,N-1\}
, and
suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Also,
we set

\displaystyle R_{a}:=\sharp\left\{(i,j,j^{\prime})\,\Big|\,1\leq i\leq r,\ 1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n,\ (d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}})_{[a]}\in\{0,p-1\}\left(\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}\right)\right\}.

(3.50)

Then,
the following two equalities hold:

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+(n-1)r+R_{a},

(3.51)

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))+R_{a}.

Proof.

To begin with, we set up some notation.
Let
U_{\oslash}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
be as in Section
2.4
.
Let
\mathscr{V}:=(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
be a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module (resp., a
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-module) such that
\psi(\nabla)=0
and
\mathcal{V}
is a vector bundle of positive rank.
In the non-resp’d case,
({{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]},{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})
is defined as in the proof of Proposition
3.11
.
In the resp’d case, we set
{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]}:=t^{p^{a}}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\right)
, which has an
S^{\mathrm{log}}
-connection
{{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}
obtained by restricting
\nabla^{[a]}
.
Then, the inclusion

{{}^{c}}\mathcal{V}^{[a]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{[a]}
restricts to an inclusion

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}:\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}).

(3.52)

On the other hand, the natural morphism

{{}^{c}}(\mathcal{V}^{\vee})^{[a]}\rightarrow{{}^{c}}(\mathcal{V}^{[a]\vee})

restricts to a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(a+1)}}
-modules

\displaystyle\beta_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}:\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})).

(3.53)

In what follows, we examine the morphisms
\alpha_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}
and
\beta_{\mathscr{V}}^{[a]}
in the case where
\mathscr{V}
is taken to be the
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash},\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)})
for

d\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
(cf. (
2.40
)).
For simplicity, write
\mathscr{O}_{d}:=\mathscr{O}_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}
and
\nabla_{d}:=\nabla_{\oslash,d}^{(N-1)}
.
First,
a straightforward calculation shows

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}_{d})=\begin{cases}t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$};\\
t^{\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}^{(a+1)}_{\oslash}&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$}.\end{cases}

(3.54)

Hence, it follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 4.4]
that
\alpha_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}^{[a]}
is injective and its cokernel satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\alpha^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}))=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$};\\
1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$}.\end{cases}

(3.55)

Also, by putting

c:=-d
, we see (from
d\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
)
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla_{d}^{\vee})^{[a]})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{c}^{[a]})\right)=\begin{cases}t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}&\text{if
$c_{[a]}\neq 0\left(\Leftrightarrow d_{[a]}\neq p-1\right)$};\\
t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}&\text{if
$c_{[a]}=0\left(\Leftrightarrow d_{[a]}=p-1\right)$}.\end{cases}

(3.56)

and that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla_{d}^{[a]})^{\vee})=t^{-\widetilde{d}_{[0,a]}+p^{a+1}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}\left(=t^{\widetilde{c}_{[0,a]}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}\right).

(3.57)

Hence,

\beta_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}^{[a]}
is injective and its cokernel satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\beta^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{d}}))=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=p-1$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq p-1$}.\end{cases}

(3.58)

The same arguments with
d
replaced by
c
give

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\alpha^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{-d}}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq p-1$};\\
1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=p-1$},\end{cases}

(3.59)

\displaystyle\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathrm{Coker}(\beta^{[a]}_{\mathscr{O}_{-d}}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $d_{[a]}=0$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\neq 0$}.\end{cases}

Now, let us return to our setting.
We set
(\mathcal{F},\nabla):=(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
.
Both
\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
and
\beta^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
become isomorphisms
when restricted to
X\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
.
Given each
d\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{d}^{[a]}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}/(t^{p^{a+1}})&\text{if $d_{[a]}\in\{0,p-1\}$};\\
0&\text{if $d_{[a]}\notin\{0,p-1\}$}.\end{cases}

(3.60)

For each
i=1,\cdots,r
, denote by
U_{i}
the formal neighborhood of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
in
X
, and by

\mathrm{incl}_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow X
the natural morphism.
Note that
\nabla
can be identified with the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\overline{\mathcal{E}nd}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
induced naturally from
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.
Hence, since
\left(\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{i,j}}\right)^{\vee}\otimes\left(\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{i,j}}\right)\cong\bigoplus_{1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq n}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{j}-d_{j}^{\prime}}
,
the restriction
(\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}},\nabla|_{U_{i}})
of
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
to
U_{i}
admits an isomorphism

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}|_{U_{i}},\nabla|_{U_{i}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{O}_{0}^{\oplus(n-1)}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq n}\mathscr{O}_{d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n}\mathscr{O}_{d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}

(3.61)

under a fixed identification
U_{i}=U_{\oslash}
.
By (
3.55
) and the first equality of (
3.59
),
the cokernel of
\alpha_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}^{[a]}
restricted to
U_{i}
is isomorphic
to

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{[a]}:=(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}/(t^{p^{a+1}}))^{\oplus(n-1)}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n}\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}.

(3.62)

This implies that
\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}
fits into the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]})\xrightarrow{\alpha^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}}\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{[a]})\rightarrow 0.

(3.63)

Here, we shall write
H^{j}(-):=H^{j}(X^{(a+1)},-)
(
j\geq 0
) for simplicity.
Since
H^{0}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))=0
(cf. (
3.36
)),
the sequence (
3.63
) yields a short exact sequence of
k
-vector spaces

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{[a]}))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))\rightarrow 0.

(3.64)

This sequence implies

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]}))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{dim}(H^{0}(\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}_{i}^{[a]})))

(3.65)

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+\sum_{i=1}^{r}(n-1+\sum_{1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n}\mathrm{length}_{\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{(a+1)}}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}))

\displaystyle=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{[a]}))+(n-1)r+R_{a}.

Thus, we have proved the first equality in (
3.51
).

Moreover, it follows from (
3.58
) and the second equality of (
3.59
) that we have the following short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{\vee[a]})\xrightarrow{\beta^{[a]}_{(\mathcal{F},\nabla)}}\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla^{[a]\vee})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\bigoplus_{1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n}\mathrm{incl}_{i*}(\mathcal{Q}^{[a]}_{d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}})\rightarrow 0.

(3.66)

Similarly to the above argument,
(
3.66
) implies the equality

\displaystyle h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{\vee})^{[a]}))=h^{1}(\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}(\nabla^{[a]\vee})))+R_{a},

(3.67)

which is precisely the second equality in (
3.51
).
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.5.
The level-reduction map

Given
a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
, we shall write
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit(N)}
for the induced
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper via level reduction.
By the definition of
p^{N}
-curvature, this
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper is dormant.
For each
\rho\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(k)^{r}
,
we shall write

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}

(3.68)

for the closed substack of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,g,r}
classifying
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-opers
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
with
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit(N)})=\rho_{i}
for every
i=1,\cdots,r
.

The assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit(N)}
determines a morphism of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
-stacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}.

(3.69)

Using this morphism, we occasionally consider
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
as a sheaf on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
with respect to the fpqc topology.

Proposition 3.14
.

Suppose that
n=2
and that
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is nonempty and connected.
Then,
the sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
locally admits a section;
that is, each geometric point of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is contained in an fpqc scheme
U
over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
such that the sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
admits a section over
U
.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
3.10
that there exists an
r
-tuple
\widetilde{\rho}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r}
with
\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}=\rho
and
\mathcal{O}p_{2,N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
.
Since both
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
are smooth and of the same dimension (cf. Theorem
3.8
, (ii)),
the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eQ2}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}

(3.70)

is flat (cf.
[
Har
, Chapter III, Exercise 10.9]
).
Moreover,
this composite is proper because of the properness of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
over
k
, so
the connectedness of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
implies that
the composite (
3.70
) is surjective.
In particular, when
regarded as a sheaf on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
with respect to the fpqc topology,

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
admits locally a section.
The same holds for
the sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
, as it contains
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
This completes the proof of this assertion.
∎

Given a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho
,
we set

\displaystyle V_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}:=\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}))),

(3.71)

i.e., the total space of the vector bundle
f_{*}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}))
.
The
S
-schemes
V_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
defined for the various pairs
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
can be glued together to form a stack

\displaystyle V_{\mathrm{univ}}

(3.72)

over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
.
That is to say, if
q
denotes the morphism
S\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
classifying
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
,
then there exists a functorial isomorphism of
S
-schemes

\displaystyle V_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\mathrm{univ}}\times_{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r},q}S.

(3.73)

In particular, it follows from Corollary
3.12
that
V_{\mathrm{univ}}
forms a relative affine space over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
whose relative dimension is
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r
.

Proposition 3.15
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
and that
the fpqc sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
locally admits a section.
Then,
the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
has a structure of relative affine space over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
modeled on
V_{\mathrm{univ}}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
is a nonempty smooth Deligne-Mumford stack over
k
of dimension
(n^{2}+2)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n)r
.

Proof.

Let
S
,
\mathscr{X}
,
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
, and
q
be as above, and choose a dormant
n^{(N+1)}
-theta characteristic
\widetilde{\vartheta}
of
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
\theta
the
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic induced by
\widetilde{\vartheta}
via level reduction, and by
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper corresponding to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

Now, suppose that we are given a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
inducing
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
via level reduction.
This corresponds to a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\widetilde{\vartheta})
-oper
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit}
, and
we obtain the composites

\displaystyle\alpha(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})

\displaystyle:\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}-1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},

(3.74)

\displaystyle\alpha(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit})

\displaystyle:\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit}}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}.

The assignment
\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\alpha(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit})
determines a map of sets

\mathcal{O}p_{q}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}}^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
,
where
\mathcal{O}p_{q}:=\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\times_{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r},q}S
.
The formation of this map
is functorial with respect to
S
, so it gives rise to a morphism of
S
-schemes

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}))).

(3.75)

Since
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
is generated by the sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}}^{(N)}
as a
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module,
this morphism allows us to view

\mathcal{O}p_{q}
as a closed subscheme of
\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})))
.

Next, the natural injection
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}-1}^{(N-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}}^{(N)}
induces a morphism between total spaces of vector bundles

\displaystyle\gamma:\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})))\rightarrow\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq p^{N}-1}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}))).

(3.76)

Let
\sigma
denote the section
S\rightarrow\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq p^{N}-1}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})))
determined by
\alpha(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
.
Then,

\mathcal{O}p_{q}
is contained in
\gamma^{-1}(\mathrm{Im}(\sigma))
.
Since
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}}^{(N)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}-1}^{(N-1)}\cong\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N}}
,
it follows from our assumption that
\gamma^{-1}(\mathrm{Im}(\sigma))

forms a relative affine space over
S
modeled on
\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})))\left(=\mathbb{V}(f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})))\right)
.

Moreover,
by Lemma
3.16
proved below,
\mathcal{O}p_{q}
forms an affine subspace of
\gamma^{-1}(\mathrm{Im}(\sigma))
, modeled on
\mathbb{V}(f_{*}^{(N)}(\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}})))
,
where
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}
denotes the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on the sheaf
\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear endomorphisms of
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
with vanishing trace induced from
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.
Since
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi})
can be naturally identified with
(\mathcal{E}nd^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}),\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}})
,
we see that
\mathcal{O}p_{q}
forms a relative affine space modeled on
V_{\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}}
.
Furthermore, it is immediately verified that this affine structure glues compatibly across
S
, yielding a global structure of relative affine space on
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
modeled on
V_{\mathrm{univ}}
via (
3.73
).
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let us retain the notation from the proof of Proposition
3.15
.
Let

\nu
be an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N}}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
(or equivalently, a global section of
\Omega^{\otimes p^{N}}\otimes\mathcal{E}nd(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
), and regard it as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}}^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
via the natural isomorphism

\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N}}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N}-1}^{(N)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N}}
.
Then,
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit}+\nu=\alpha(\widetilde{\nabla}_{1}^{\diamondsuit})
for some
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\widetilde{\vartheta})
-oper
\widetilde{\nabla}_{1}^{\diamondsuit}
if and only if
\nu\in H^{0}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}))
.
Moreover, if these equivalent conditions are fulfilled, then the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\widetilde{\vartheta})
-oper “
\widetilde{\nabla}_{1}^{\diamondsuit}
” is uniquely determined.

Proof.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit}+\nu=\alpha(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\diamondsuit}_{1})
for some
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\widetilde{\vartheta})
-oper
\widetilde{\nabla}_{1}
.
Then, by the commutativity relation (
2.4
),
\nu
must lie in
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\mathcal{E}nd^{0}}))
.
Moreover, by reversing this discussion, we see that the inverse direction of the desired equivalence can be verified.
∎

3.6.
An affine space structure on
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}

Denote by
{{}^{\dagger}}\mathfrak{c}_{N+1}
the subscheme of
\mathfrak{c}_{N+1}
defined to be the scheme-theoretic image of the closed subscheme

\displaystyle\left\{(v_{1},\cdots,v_{N+1})\in\mathfrak{g}^{N+1}\,|\,[v_{j},v_{j^{\prime}}]=0\ \text{if}\ j\neq j^{\prime}\right\}

(3.77)

under the natural quotient
\mathfrak{g}^{N+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{c}_{N+1}
.
We shall set
{{}^{\dagger}}\Xi_{n,N+1}
to be the
k
-scheme fitting into the following Cartesian square diagram:

\textstyle{{{}^{\dagger}}\Xi_{n,N+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{}^{\dagger}}\mathfrak{c}_{N+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Xi_{n,N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{c}_{N},}

where the bottom horizontal arrow arises from (
3.17
) and the right-hand vertical arrow is induced by the projection onto the first
N
factors
\mathfrak{g}^{N+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{N}
.
We denote the left-hand vertical arrow by

\displaystyle{{}^{\dagger}}\pi:{{}^{\dagger}}\Xi_{n,N+1}\twoheadrightarrow\Xi_{n,N}.

(3.82)

In particular, for each element
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{n,N}
represented by a multiset
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
, the inverse image
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})
can be identified, after choosing an ordering of the elements in
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
, with the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}
of the maximal torus of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
consisting of diagonal matrices.

Let
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an element of
\Xi^{r}_{n,N}
satisfying
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
, and write
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho):=({{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{i}))_{i=1}^{r}
.
Since the constituents in the monodromy operators of a
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure commute pairwise,
the radii
\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
of a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
defines a point of
{{}^{\dagger}}\mathfrak{c}_{N}
.
Moreover, if
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
is classified by
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
,
then
the
r
-tuple
\rho(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}):=(\rho_{i}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}))_{i=1}^{r}
lies in
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
.
The assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\rho(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})
determines a morphism of
k
-stacks

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rad}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\rightarrow{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho).

(3.83)

For
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)(k)
,
the inverse image
\mathrm{Rad}^{-1}(\widetilde{\rho})
can be identified with
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}
.

Now, fix a
k
-rational point
q\in\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}(k)
.
Then,
\mathrm{Rad}
restricts to a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}|_{q}\rightarrow{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho),

(3.84)

where
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}|_{q}:=\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\times_{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r},q}\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
In what follows, we examine an affine structure on each fiber of
\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}
.

Let
S
,
\mathscr{X}
be as before, and
suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
and the point
q
lies over the point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying
\mathscr{X}
.
Also, choose a dormant
n^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
\mathscr{X}
.
The point
q
determines a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
, which corresponds to
a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.
Let us fix
i\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
and fix an identification of the formal neighborhood
U_{i}
of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})\subseteq X
with the formal disk
U_{\oslash}
over
k
.
Then, there exists a representative
[d_{i,1},\cdots,d_{i,n}]\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}\backslash(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{n}
of
\rho_{i}
such that

0\leq\widetilde{d}_{i,1}<\widetilde{d}_{i,2}<\cdots<\widetilde{d}_{i,n}<p^{N}
and
we have an isomorphism
of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash}
-modules
(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit})|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{j}}
.
Similarly to the discussion surrounding (
3.61
),
the restriction of
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
to
U_{i}
admits an isomorphism

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{t}\otimes_{k}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,0}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq n}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq j^{\prime}<j\leq n}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}},

(3.85)

since
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\phi}
can be identified with the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\overline{\mathcal{E}nd}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta})
induced naturally from

\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
.

We now denote by
(\mathcal{V},\nabla_{\mathcal{V}})
the codomain of this isomorphism.
The inclusion into the first summand
\mathfrak{t}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}
induces a
k
-linear injection
\iota:\sigma_{i*}(\mathfrak{t})\hookrightarrow\sigma_{i*}(\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{V}))
.
Since
\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{i,j}-d_{i,j^{\prime}}}\not\cong\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,0}
for
j\neq j^{\prime}
,
it follows from Lemma
2.3
that the image of
the composite

\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}\twoheadrightarrow\sigma_{i*}(\sigma^{*}_{i}(\mathcal{V}))

coincides with
\mathrm{Im}(\iota)
.
It follows that (
3.85
) induces a surjection
\xi_{i}:\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\twoheadrightarrow\left(\sigma_{i*}(\mathfrak{t})=\right)\sigma_{i*}^{(N)}(\mathfrak{t})
, and we obtain a composite

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}_{i}:\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{\Omega^{(N)}}\otimes\xi_{i}}\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\sigma^{(N)}_{i*}(\mathfrak{t})\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{(N)}_{i*}(\sigma^{(N)*}_{i}(\Omega^{(N)})\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{t})\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{(N)}_{i*}(\mathfrak{t}),

(3.86)

where the final arrow arises from the residue isomorphism
\sigma^{(N)*}_{i}(\Omega^{(N)})\xrightarrow{\sim}k
.

By setting
\mathcal{G}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Ker}(\xi^{\prime}_{i})
, we obtain the following short exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}
-modules

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i*}^{(N)}(\mathfrak{t})\rightarrow 0.

(3.87)

Note that both the sheaf
\mathcal{G}
and
the second arrow
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})
in this sequence do not depend on the choice of the isomorphism
(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla^{\diamondsuit})|_{U_{i}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathscr{O}^{(N-1)}_{\oslash,d_{j}}
.

Lemma 3.17
.

The sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G})\rightarrow H^{0}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}^{r}\rightarrow 0

(3.88)

obtained from (
3.87
) by applying the
\delta
-functor
H^{*}(X^{(N)},-)
(under the natural identification
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\sigma^{(N)}_{i*}(\mathfrak{t}))=\mathfrak{t}
) is exact.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}_{k}(H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G}))=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r.

(3.89)

Proof.

We begin by establishing the exactness of (
3.88
).
Let us use the notation “
{{}^{c}}(-)
” as in Proposition
3.11
, (ii).
The Cartier operator of
({{}^{c}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[N-1]},{{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})
induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{Coker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})

(cf.
[
Ogu2
, Theorem 3.1.1]
or the discussion following
[
Ogu1
, Chapter I, Proposition 1.2.4]
).
It follows that

\displaystyle H^{1}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]}))\cong H^{1}(X^{(N)},\mathrm{Coker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]}))=0,

(3.90)

where the second equality follows from
Proposition
3.11
, (ii).
On the other hand,
Lemma
2.3
in the case
m=0

implies

\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})\subseteq\bigcap_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Ker}(\xi_{i})
.
This inclusion yields
an injection
\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}({{}^{c}}\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}[N-1]})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}
, which is an isomorphism over
X^{(N)}\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Im}(\sigma^{(N)}_{i})
.
Hence, (
3.90
) implies

H^{1}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G})=0
, and hence the sequence (
3.88
) is exact.
The second assertion follows from the exactness of (
3.88
) and Corollary
3.12
.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
and that the fpqc sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
on
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
admits locally a section.
Let us fix
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
, and write

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}|_{q}:=\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\times_{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r},q}\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Then,
the affine structure on
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}|_{q}
(modeled on the
k
-vector space
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
) resulting from Proposition
3.15

restricts to
an affine structure on
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}|_{q}
modelded on
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G})
via the inclusion
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(X^{(N)},\Omega^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the various definitions involved.
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

Let us keep the assumption stated in Proposition
3.18
.
Then,
the morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\times_{k}{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)

(3.91)

induced by (
3.69
) and
\mathrm{Rad}
is smooth, surjective, and of relative dimension
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r
.
In particular, for each
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
,
the projection
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is smooth, surjective, and of relative dimension
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r
.

Proof.

Let
q
be as before.
Suppose, on the contrary, that

\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}
is not surjective.
Since
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
is irreducible,
the scheme-theoretic image of
\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}

must be of strictly smaller dimension.
It follows that the dimension of any nonempty fiber of
\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}
must have dimension strictly greater than

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}|_{q})-\mathrm{dim}({{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho))

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r-(n-1)r

(3.92)

\displaystyle=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r

(3.93)

(cf.
[
Har
, Chapter II, Exercise 3.22]
), where the first equality follows from Proposition
3.15
.
On the other hand, the fiber of
\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}
over
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}|_{q}
,
whose dimension is computed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}|_{q})=\mathrm{dim}(H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G}))=(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r,

(3.94)

where the first equality follows from Proposition
3.18
and the second equality follows from Lemma
3.17
.
Thus, we obtain a contradiction, which implies the surjectivity of
\mathrm{Rad}|_{q}
.

By applying the above discussion to every geometric point
q\in\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}(k)
, we conclude that the morphism (
3.91
) is surjective and that all its fibers are affine spaces of dimension
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r
.
Hence, the required assertion follows from the smoothness of

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
and
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)
(cf. Proposition
3.15
and
[
Har
, Chapter III, Exercise 10.9]
).
∎

Corollary 3.20
.

Assume the hypothesis of
Proposition
3.18
.
Then, for each
\widetilde{\rho}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{3}
,
the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\widetilde{\rho},0,3}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Propositions
3.18
and
3.19
, since the equality
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{G})=0
holds when
(g,r,2)=(0,3,2)
.
∎

3.7.
The Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism of higher level

Recall the
S
-scheme
B_{\mathscr{X}}^{\flat}
(resp.,
B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X},0}
) introduced in Section
2.2
.
Under the situation where
m=N
, these schemes defined for the various
\mathscr{X}
’s can be glued together to form a stack

\displaystyle B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ},0}\right)

(3.95)

over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.
That is to say, if
\overline{q}
denotes the morphism
S\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
classifying
\mathscr{X}
,
then we have
B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r},\overline{q}}S\cong B^{\flat}_{\mathscr{X}}
(resp.,
B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ},0}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r},\overline{q}}S\cong B_{\mathscr{X},0}^{\flat}
).
By
(
2.14
) (resp., (
2.25
)),
the stack
B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}
(resp.,
B_{\mathrm{univ},0}^{\flat}
) forms a relative affine space over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
whose relative dimension equals
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n^{2}+n-2)r
(resp.,
(n^{2}-1)(g-1)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-1)r
).

For each
\rho\in\mathfrak{c}_{N}(k)^{r}
(where
\rho=\emptyset
if
r=0
),
the assignment
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\psi\text{-}\mathrm{Char}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit})

(cf. (
3.8
)) yields a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\rightarrow B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ}},

(3.96)

which makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}}
(
3.69
)
\textstyle{B^{\flat}_{\mathrm{univ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{N,\rho,g,r}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.}

The following assertion generalizes
[
BeTr
, Theorem A.1]
,
[
JoPa
, Theorem 6.1.3]
,
[
Moc1
, Chapter II, Theorem 2.3]
, and
[
Wak4
, Theorem 3.39]
.

Theorem 3.21
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
and that the fpqc sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
on
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
admits locally a section.
Then, the following assertions hold:

(i)

The morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}

(3.101)

induced by (
3.7
)
is finite and faithfully flat.

(ii)

Let
\widetilde{\rho}
be an element of
{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)\cap\Xi_{n,N+1}^{r}
.
(By Proposition
3.19
, the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}
is nonempty.)
Then,
\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}
restricts to a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}_{\widetilde{\rho}}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\rightarrow B_{\mathrm{univ},0}^{\flat},

(3.102)

and the induced morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}B_{\mathrm{univ},0}^{\flat}

(3.103)

is finite and faithfully flat.

Proof.

Let us consider assertion (i).
To begin with, we shall prove
the properness of (
3.101
) restricted over each geometric point of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
, by applying the standard valuative criterion.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be as above, and suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Also, let
R
be a valuation ring of a field
K
over
k
whose residue field
R/\mathfrak{m}
is isomorphic to
k
.
Denote by
\eta
(resp.,
s
) the generic (resp., closed) point of
T:=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
.
For
\Box\in\{T,\eta,s\}
, we denote by
\mathscr{X}_{\Box}:=(X_{\Box},\{\sigma_{\Box,i}\}_{i})
the base-change of
\mathscr{X}
over
\Box
(in particular,
\mathscr{X}_{s}=\mathscr{X}
).
The sheaf of logarithmic
1
-forms (resp., logarithmic tangent fields; resp., logarithmic differential operators of level
N
) on the associated log curve
X_{\Box}^{\mathrm{log}}/\Box^{\mathrm{log}}
is denoted by
\Omega_{\Box}
(resp.,
\mathcal{T}_{\Box}
; resp.,
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{D}_{\leq j,\Box}^{(N)}
).
According to
[
Wak14
, Proposition 5.14]
,
there exists an
n^{(N+1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X^{\mathrm{log}}/\mathrm{Spec}(k)^{\mathrm{log}}
.
In particular, we obtain the base-change
\vartheta_{\Box}:=(\varTheta_{\Box},\nabla_{\vartheta,\Box})
(resp.,
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}
) of
\vartheta
(resp.,
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
) over
\Box
.

Let us take a
T
-rational point of
B_{\mathscr{X}}
, which corresponds, via (
2.13
),
to a polynomial

Q_{T}:=t^{n}+a_{2}t^{n-2}+\cdots+a_{n}
for some
a_{\ell}\in H^{0}(X_{T}^{(N+1)},(\Omega_{T}^{(N+1)})^{\otimes\ell})\subseteq H^{0}(X_{T},\Omega_{T}^{\otimes p^{N+1}\cdot\ell})
(
2\leq\ell\leq n
).
Write
Q_{\eta}:=Q_{T}|_{X_{\eta}}
,
Q_{s}:=Q_{T}|_{X_{s}}
, and
suppose that we are given
a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\eta}

on
\mathscr{X}_{\eta}
satisfying
\psi\text{-}\mathrm{Char}(\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\eta})=Q_{\eta}
.
Let
\nabla_{\eta}^{\diamondsuit}
denote the
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\vartheta_{\eta})
-oper
corresponding to
\mathscr{E}_{\eta}^{\spadesuit}
.
To prove the desired properness, it suffices to construct a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{T}^{\spadesuit}
on
\mathscr{X}_{T}

whose
generic fiber is
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{\eta}
and which satisfies the equality
\psi\text{-}\mathrm{Char}(\mathscr{E}_{T}^{\spadesuit})=Q_{T}
.

For
\Box\in\{T,\eta,s\}
,
let
\langle Q_{\Box}\rangle
be the
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}
-submodule of
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}\otimes\varTheta_{\Box}
generated by the sections

\displaystyle\left((\partial^{\langle p^{N+1}\rangle})^{n}+\langle a_{2}|_{X_{\Box}},(\partial^{\langle p^{N+1}\rangle})^{\otimes 2}\rangle\cdot(\partial^{\langle p^{N+1}\rangle})^{n-2}+\cdots+\langle a_{n}|_{X_{\Box}},(\partial^{\langle p^{N+1}\rangle})^{\otimes n}\rangle\right)\otimes v

(3.104)

defined for various local sections
v\in\varTheta_{\Box}
, where
\langle-,-\rangle
’s denote the natural pairings
\Omega_{\Box}^{\otimes p^{N+1}\cdot\ell}\times\mathcal{T}^{\otimes p^{N+1}\cdot\ell}_{\Box}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{\Box}}
, and
\partial^{\langle p^{N+1}\rangle}
denotes the local section of
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}
as introduced in Section
2.1
.
The quotient
\mathcal{P}_{\Box}:=(\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}\otimes\varTheta_{\Box})/\langle Q_{\Box}\rangle
carries a natural
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\Box}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{\Box}}
.
For each
j=0,\cdots,p^{N+1}\cdot n
, we shall set
\mathcal{P}_{\Box}^{j}
to be the subbundle of
\mathcal{P}_{\Box}
defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Box}^{j}:=\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq p^{N+1}\cdot n-j-1,\Box}\otimes\varTheta_{\Box}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}_{\Box}^{(N)}\otimes\varTheta_{\Box}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{quotient}}\mathcal{P}_{\Box}\right),

(3.105)

where
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{\leq-1,\Box}:=0
.
The composite of natural morphisms
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\Box}^{(N)}\otimes\varTheta_{\Box}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Box}
is injective
and restricts, for each
j=0,\cdots,n
, to an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}^{j}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}_{\Box}^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n+j}
.
We regard
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\Box}}
-submodule of
\mathcal{P}_{\Box}
via the composite injection
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\Box}
.
In the case
\Box=\eta
,
the composite of the inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{(N)}\otimes\varTheta_{\eta}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta}
and
the morphism
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{(N)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta}
arising from
\nabla_{\eta}^{\diamondsuit}

factors through
the projection
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{(N)}\otimes\varTheta_{\eta}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\eta}
, as the equality
\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla_{\eta}^{\diamondsuit}))=Q_{\eta}
holds.
As a result, we obtain a surjection between
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-modules
\varpi_{\eta}:(\mathcal{P}_{\eta},\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}})\twoheadrightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta},\nabla_{\eta}^{\diamondsuit})
.

By the properness of Quot schemes,
\varpi_{\eta}
extends to a surjection
\varpi_{T}:\mathcal{P}_{T}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
for some coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X_{T}}
-module
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
that is flat over
T
and satisfies
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta})
,
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\mathrm{rdeg}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\eta})
.
Note that the composite

\displaystyle h:\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,T}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{P}_{T}\xrightarrow{\varpi_{T}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

(3.106)

is injective, since
its generic fiber
h_{\eta}
is an isomorphism and
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,T}
is locally free.
The kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{\eta})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})|_{X_{\eta}}\right)
of
\varpi_{\eta}
is closed under
\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta}}
,
so
the
T
-flatness of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
implies that
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})
must be closed under
\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{T}}
.
Hence, there exists a
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
that extends
\nabla_{\eta}^{\diamondsuit}
and commutes with
\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{T}}
via
\varpi_{T}
.

By an argument entirely similar to the proof of (the first assertion in)
[
Wak14
, Lemma 5.25]
, the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is verified to be locally free.
It follows that

\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})
defines a subbundle of
\mathcal{P}_{T}
and
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})|_{X_{s}}
coincides, in
\mathcal{P}_{s}
, with the kernel
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{s})
of the special fiber
\varpi_{s}:\mathcal{P}_{s}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{X_{s}}
of
\varpi_{T}
.
For each
j=0,\cdots,p^{N+1}\cdot n-n
,
we set
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}:=\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})\cap\mathcal{P}_{T}^{j}
.
Since the composite
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})|_{X_{\eta}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\eta}/\mathcal{P}_{\eta}^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n}
is an isomorphism,
the identity
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n}=0
holds.
The subquotient
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}/\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j+1}
is contained in
\mathcal{P}_{T}^{j}/\mathcal{P}_{T}^{j+1}
,
so it is
T
-flat.
Hence, the collection
\{\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}|_{X_{s}}\}_{j=0}^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n}
forms a decreasing filtration on
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{s})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})|_{X_{s}}\right)
and, for each
j=0,\cdots,p^{N+1}\cdot n-n-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{deg}\left((\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}|_{X_{s}})/(\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j+1}|_{X_{s}})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{deg}\left((\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}|_{X_{\eta}})/\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{T})^{j}|_{X_{\eta}}\right)

(3.107)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{P}_{\eta}^{j}/\mathcal{P}_{\eta}^{j+1})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{T}^{\otimes(p^{N+1}\cdot n-1-j)}_{\eta}\otimes\varTheta_{\eta})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{deg}(\varTheta)-(2g-2+r)(p^{N+1}\cdot n-1-j)

\displaystyle<\mathrm{deg}(\varTheta)-(2g-2+r)(n-1).

(3.108)

Suppose now that the special fiber
h_{s}:\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,s}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{X_{s}}
of
h
is
not
injective.
Similarly to the second assertion in
[
Wak14
, Lemma 5.25]
,
there exists a line subbundle of
\mathrm{Ker}(h_{s})
whose degree is equal to or greater than the value
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,s}^{0}/\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,s}^{1})=\mathrm{deg}(\varTheta)-(2g-2+r)(n-1)
.
Note that
\mathrm{Ker}(h_{s})
can be identified with
\mathrm{Ker}(\varpi_{s})\cap\mathcal{P}_{s}^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n}
via the isomorphism “
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,\Box}^{j}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}_{\Box}^{p^{N+1}\cdot n-n+j}
” constructed above in the case where
(\Box,j)
is taken to be
(s,0)
.
Hence, we obtain a contradiction because of the computation in the sequence (
3.107
).
Thus,
h_{s}
must be injective.
By comparing the degrees of
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,s}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{X_{s}}
, we see that
h_{s}
, and hence
h
, is an isomorphism.

Under the identification
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,T}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
given by the isomorphism
h
,
the
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}_{T}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
constructed above determines
a
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure
\nabla_{T}^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,T}
.
It is immediately verified that
\nabla_{T}^{\diamondsuit}

forms a
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}^{(N+1)},\vartheta_{T})
-oper on
\mathscr{X}_{T}
.
Since
\varpi_{T}
defines a surjection between
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-modules
(\mathcal{P}_{T},\nabla_{\mathcal{P}_{T}})\twoheadrightarrow(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta,T},\nabla_{T}^{\diamondsuit})
,
the equality
\mathrm{Char}(\breve{\psi}(\nabla_{T}^{\diamondsuit}))=Q_{T}
holds.
Hence, the
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N+1)}
-oper corresponding to
\nabla_{T}^{\diamondsuit}
satisfies the required conditions, and this proves the properness of (
3.101
) over every geometric point of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.

We now recall that both
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
and
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}
are relative affines spaces (in particular, smooth) over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
of the same relative dimension (cf. Proposition
3.15
).
Combining this fact with the properness established above,
we see that (
3.101
) is proper, and hence finite.
Also, it follows from
[
Har
, Chapter III, Exercise 10.9]
that
this morphism is also faithfully flat.
This completes the proof of assertion (i).

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
The first assertion follows from
[
Wak4
, Eq. (345)]
and the observation in the proof of Proposition
2.2
.
The second assertion follows from assertion (i) and the fact that both the domain and codomain of (
3.103
) are smooth over
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
of the same relative dimension (cf. Proposition
3.19
).
∎

Definition 3.22
.

The morphisms
\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}
and
\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}_{\widetilde{\rho}}
introduced above are called the
level-
N
Hitchin-Mochizuki morphism

(cf.
[
BeTr
, Appendix A]
,
[
LasPa1
, Section 3]
,
[
Moc1
, Chapter II, Section 2]
,
[
Wak4
, Section 3]
for the case
N=1
).

It follows from Proposition
3.7
and the definition of
p^{N+1}
-nilpotency that
the inverse image
(\mathrm{HM}^{(N)})^{-1}(0_{\mathrm{univ}})
of the zero section
0_{\mathrm{univ}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}\rightarrow\left(B_{\mathrm{univ},0}^{\flat}\subseteq\right)B_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\flat}
via
\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}
admits
a decomposition

\displaystyle(\mathrm{HM}^{(N)})^{-1}(0_{\mathrm{univ}})=\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r},

(3.109)

where the disjoint union runs over the elements
\widetilde{\rho}
in
\Xi_{n,N+1}^{r}
with
\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}=\rho
, i.e.,
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)\cap\Xi_{n,N}^{r}
.
If we fix such an element
\widetilde{\rho}
,
then
the restriction of (
3.69
) to
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
defines a morphism

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}:\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}

(3.110)

over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.
Since
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
can be identified with
(\mathrm{HM}^{(N)}_{\widetilde{\rho}})^{-1}(0_{\mathrm{univ}})
(cf. (
3.102
)),
Theorem
3.21
, (ii), implies the following assertion.

Theorem 3.23
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
and that
the fpqc sheaf
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\Rightarrow\rho,g,r}
on
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
admits locally a section.
Then,
for any
\widetilde{\rho}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho)\cap\Xi_{n,N}^{r}
,
the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is nonempty, and
the morphism
\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}

is
finite and faithfully flat.

Corollary 3.24
.

Suppose that
n=2
.
Then, for any
\widetilde{\rho}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{3}
with
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)},0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
,
the stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\rho,0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
3.23
that
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\rho,0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is nonempty.
Hence, the assertion follows from
the fact that
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\rho,0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is a closed subscheme of the
k
-scheme
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\rho,0,3}
, which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
by Corollary
3.20
.
∎

4.
The irreducibility of the moduli space of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
A
(cf. Theorem
4.6
).
Our argument is basically an extension of the proof given by S. Mochizuki for
N=1
(cf.
[
Moc2
, Chapter II, Theorems 1.12 and 2.8]
).
We begin by establishing the irreducibility of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
under the assumption that
\mathrm{dim}(\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}})=1
(cf. Lemma
4.4
).
Then, we proceed by induction, reducing the general case to lower-dimensional cases via an analysis of divisors on the moduli space defined as the images of various gluing morphisms (cf. Lemma
4.5
).

Throughout this section, we assume that
n=2
.

4.1.
Splittings induced from dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

Let
(g,r)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
2g-2+r>0
,

S
a scheme (or more generally, a Deligne-Mumford stack) over
k
,
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
an
r
-pointed stable curve of genus
g
over
S
, and

\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})

a dormant
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic of
\mathscr{X}
.
Also, let us take
a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
.
Denote by
D
the relative effective divisor on
X
defined as the union of the
\sigma_{i}
’s.

Consider the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\xrightarrow{\nabla^{\diamondsuit}}\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},

(4.1)

where the first arrow arises from the inclusions
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
and
\varTheta\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}
.
This composite becomes an isomorphism when restricted to
\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}=\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\left(\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\right)
,
and hence determines a split surjection

\displaystyle\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}):\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}

(4.2)

of the natural short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta\rightarrow(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})\otimes\varTheta\rightarrow 0.

(4.3)

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose that
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
, and that we are given two dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-opers
\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit}
,
\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{X}
.
Then,
\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit}=\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit}
if and only if
\delta(\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit})=\delta(\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit})
.

Proof.

Since the morphism (
3.4
) for
j=0
associated to each
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper is an isomorphism,
the assertion follows immediately from the fact that
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
is generated by sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}
as a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module.
∎

For each
i=1,\cdots,r
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
the isomorphism (
2.45
) restricts to an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

\sigma^{*}_{i}({{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq m})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq m}
,
where
\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq m}:=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot\partial_{\mathcal{B}}^{\langle j\rangle}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{S}\right)
.
This isomorphism allows us to identify
\sigma_{i}^{*}(\mathcal{D}_{\leq m}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta)
with
\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq m}\otimes\sigma_{i}^{*}(\varTheta)
.
Hence,
the pull-back of
\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
via
\sigma_{i}
determines a morphism

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}):=\sigma_{i}^{*}(\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}))\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq p^{N-1}}\otimes\sigma_{i}^{*}(\varTheta),\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq 1}\otimes\sigma_{i}^{*}(\varTheta)).

(4.4)

Proposition 4.2
.

Let us fix
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
(
=((\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\})^{r}
via (
3.15
)).
Suppose that, for each
j=1,2
, we are given
a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{j}
on
\mathscr{X}
of radii
\rho
such that (the corresponding
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper of) the
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)},\vartheta)
-oper obtained from
\nabla_{j}^{\diamondsuit}
by reducing its level is normal, in the sense of
[
Wak4
, Definition 4.53]
.
Then,
the following assertions hold:

(i)

For each
i=1,\cdots,r
,
the equality
\mu(\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}=\mu(\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
holds.

(ii)

For each
i=1,\cdots,r
, the equality

\kappa_{i}(\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit})=\kappa_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{2})
holds.
In particular, the difference
\delta(\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit})-\delta(\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit})
specifies an
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-linear morphism

(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)}/\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq 1})\otimes\varTheta\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\varTheta(-D)
.

Proof.

First, we shall consider assertion (i).
After possibly replacing
S
with its covering,
we can assume that
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
is defined by some function
t
defined on its open neighborhood; it determines an identification of
U_{\oslash}
with the formal neighborhood of
\mathrm{Im}(\sigma_{i})
.

If
c
denotes the exponent of
\nabla_{\vartheta}
at
\sigma_{i}
, then there exists a subset
\{a,b\}
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
a\neq b
,
a+b=c
, and
\overline{\left(\frac{a-b}{2}\right)}=\rho_{i}
in
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}
.
For
j=1,2
,
there exists an isomorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{\oslash}^{(N-1)}
-modules

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}:(\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta},\nabla_{j}^{\diamondsuit})|_{U_{\oslash}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus 2},\nabla_{\oslash,a}^{(N-1)}\oplus\nabla_{\oslash,b}^{(N-1)}).

(4.5)

It follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 6.3, (i)]
that the image of
\varTheta\left(\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{\varTheta}\right)
under
\alpha_{j}
is generated by an element
(v_{j}(t),u_{j}(t))
of
H^{0}(U_{\oslash},(\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\times})^{\oplus 2})
, satisfying
v_{j}(0),u_{j}(0)\in H^{0}(S,\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\times})
.
After possibly composing
\alpha_{j}
with an automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\oslash}^{\oplus 2}
,
we can assume that
v_{j}(0)=u_{j}(0)=1
.
The restriction of

\alpha_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{1}
to
\sigma_{i}
defines
an automorphism
\overline{\alpha}_{21}
of
\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq 1}\otimes\sigma_{i}^{*}(\varTheta)
.
By the above assumption, the
subbundle
\sigma^{*}_{i}(\varTheta)\left(=\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq 0}\otimes\sigma^{*}_{i}(\varTheta)\right)
of
\mathcal{B}_{S,\leq 1}\otimes\sigma^{*}_{i}(\varTheta)

is preserved by this automorphism.
If
P
denotes the upper-triangular invertible
2\times 2
matrix corresponding to
\overline{\alpha}_{21}
,
then
the equality
P^{-1}\mu_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{1})^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}P=\mu_{i}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}_{2})^{\langle p^{a}\rangle}
holds for every
a=0,\cdots,N-1
.
Since we have assumed that both
\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit}
and
\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit}
are normal,

P
must be a scalar matrix.
This implies

\mu_{i}(\nabla_{1}^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}=\mu_{i}(\nabla_{2}^{\diamondsuit})^{\langle\bullet\rangle}
, completing
the proof of assertion (i).

Finally, assertion (ii) follows from assertion (i) and the definition of
\kappa_{i}(-)
.
∎

4.2.
The irreducibility of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
for
(g,r)=(0,4)
,
(1,1)

As the first step in the proof of Theorem
A
,
we consider the case where the moduli space is of dimension
1
.
Let us keep the notation
from the previous subsection,
To begin with, we prove the following assertion.

Proposition 4.3
.

We suppose that
(g,r)=(0,4)
or
(1,1)
(or equivalently,
\mathrm{dim}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r})=1
), and that the classifying morphism
S\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
of
\mathscr{X}
is finite and faithfully flat.
Then, for each positive integer
\ell
,
the vector bundle
f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes\ell}(-D))
does not admit any subbundle isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

Proof.

Denote by
\omega
the canonical bundle on
X/S
(hence
\Omega(-D)\cong\omega
).
Let us consider a decreasing filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{j}\}_{j=0}^{\ell}
on
\Omega^{\otimes\ell}(-D)
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{j}:=\Omega^{\otimes\ell}((-j-1)D)\left(\cong\omega^{\otimes\ell}((\ell-j-1)D)\right)

(4.6)

for
j=0,\cdots,\ell-1
and
\mathcal{F}^{\ell}:=0
.
This induces a decreasing filtration
\{f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j})\}_{j=0}^{\ell}
on the direct image
f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes\ell}(-D))
.
For each
j=0,\cdots,\ell-2
, we have

\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1})=(2g-2)\ell+(\ell-j-1)r>2g-2
, which implies
\mathbb{R}^{1}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1})=0
.
It follows that

\displaystyle f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j})/f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1})

\displaystyle\cong f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j}/\mathcal{F}^{j+1})

\displaystyle\cong f_{*}\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i*}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\omega^{\otimes\ell}((\ell-j-1)D)))\right)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}^{*}(\omega^{\otimes\ell}(\ell-j-1)D)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}^{*}(\omega)^{\otimes(j+1)},

where the last “
\cong
” follows from the residue isomorphism
\sigma^{*}_{i}(\omega(D))\left(\cong\sigma_{i}^{*}(\Omega)\right)\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}
.

Recall the following sequence of equalities:

\displaystyle c_{1}(\sigma_{i}^{*}(\omega)^{\otimes(j+1)})=(j+1)\psi_{i}=\begin{cases}(j+1)\cdot\mathrm{deg}(S/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r})&\text{if $(g,r)=(0,4)$};\\
\frac{1}{24}\cdot(j+1)\cdot\mathrm{deg}(S/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r})&\text{if $(g,r)=(1,1)$,}\end{cases}

(4.7)

where
\psi_{i}
denotes the pull-back over
S
of the
i
-th psi class on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
(cf.
[
ACG
, Chapter 17, Lemma 7.4, and Chapter 19, Eq.(̇7.8)]
).
It follows that, for any
j=0,\cdots,\ell-2
, there is no line subbundle of
f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j})/f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{j+1})
isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
If
(g,r)=(0,4)
, then
f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\ell-1})=f_{*}(\omega^{\otimes\ell})=0
.
On the
other hand, if
(g,r)=(1,1)
, then
f_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\ell-1})
is the pull-back of the
(\ell-1)
-st tensor power of the Hodge bundle
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{1,1}
, which is not isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
From these considerations,
f_{*}(\Omega^{\otimes\ell}(-D))
does not admit any line subbundle isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

By applying the above proposition,
we obtain the following assertion.

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose that
(g,r)=(1,1)
or
(0,4)
.
Then, for
\rho\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
, the moduli stack
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
(in the case
n=2
)
is connected (and hence, irreducible), provided it is nonempty.

Proof.

With the notation as above, we assume further that
S=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
and
\mathscr{X}
is taken to be the universal family of curves.
Also, write
S^{\prime}:=\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
.
For any stack
Q
over
S
, we use the subscript “
Q
” to denote the result of base-changing each object over
S
to
Q
.
Let us take a dormant
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
\mathscr{X}
.
(It follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 5.14]
that such a
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic always exists.)
By
[
Wak4
, Proposition 4.55]
,
there exists a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta_{S^{\prime}})
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathscr{X}_{S^{\prime}}
corresponding to
the universal family of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
\mathscr{X}_{S^{\prime}}
such that the induced
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper is normal, in the sense of
[
Wak4
, Definition 4.53]
.

Now, suppose that there exist connected components
S_{1}
,
S_{2}
of
S^{\prime}
with
S_{1}\cap S_{2}=\emptyset
.
We can choose a
smooth proper
Deligne-Mumford stack
T
of dimension
1
that dominates both
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
as a stack over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
, i.e., fits into a commutative diagram of

k
-stacks

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.}

In particular,
T
is finite and faithfully flat over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}
.

For each
i=1,2
,
let
h_{i}
be the composite

h_{i}:T\rightarrow S_{i}\hookrightarrow S^{\prime}
.
The pull-back
h^{*}_{i}(\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}))
of
\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
(cf. (
4.2
))
determines a morphism

(\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}
.
The difference
h^{*}_{1}(\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}))-h^{*}_{2}(\delta(\nabla^{\diamondsuit}))
vanishes when restricted to
(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}
, and thus determines a morphism

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}:((\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)})_{T}/(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T})\otimes\varTheta_{T}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}(-D_{T})

(4.12)

(cf. Proposition
4.2
, (ii)).
We abuse notation by writing
\delta^{\prime}
for the corresponding
global section of the vector bundle

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle:=f_{T*}((\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}(-D_{T})\otimes(((\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)})_{T}/(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T})\otimes\varTheta_{T})^{\vee})

(4.13)

\displaystyle\left(\cong f_{T*}(\mathcal{H}om(((\mathcal{D}_{\leq p^{N-1}}^{(N-1)})_{T}/(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T})\otimes\varTheta_{T},(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)})_{T}\otimes\varTheta_{T}(-D_{T})))\right)

on
T
.

Since
S_{1}\cap S_{2}=\emptyset
,
it follows from Lemma
4.1
that
the image of the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
determined by
\delta^{\prime}
specifies a line subbundle of
\mathcal{V}
.
On the other hand, one can verify from

\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq j}/\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq j-1}\cong\mathcal{T}^{\otimes j}
that the vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
admits a filtration whose graded pieces are each isomorphic to
f_{T*}(\Omega^{\otimes\ell}(-D)_{T})
for
\ell>0
.
This contradicts Proposition
4.3
applied to the case where “
S
” is taken to be
T
.
That is to say,

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\left(=S^{\prime}\right)
must be connected, which completes the proof of this assertion.
∎

4.3.
The irreducibility of
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
for general
(g,r)

To handle the general case of pairs
(g,r)
, we consider gluing morphisms on moduli stacks of various types of higher-level
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers.
For the case of dormant opers, we refer to
[
Wak14
, Example 6.31]
.

Let
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
,
r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
be nonnegative integers with
2g_{i}-1+r_{i}>0
(
i=1,2
) and
g=g_{1}+g_{2}
,
r=r_{1}+r_{2}
.
These integers determine the gluing morphism

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{2},r_{2}+1}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1}+g_{2},r_{1}+r_{2}}

(4.14)

obtained by attaching the respective last marked points of curves classified by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1},r_{1}+1}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{2},r_{2}+1}
to form a node.

For
\rho_{1}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r_{1}}
,
\rho_{2}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r_{2}}
, and
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}
,
there exists a morphism

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{0}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{2},\rho_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{2}),g,r}

(4.15)

obtained by gluing together a pair of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers classified by its domain along the fibers over the respective last marked points of the underlying curves (cf.
[
Wak14
, Example 6.31, (i)]
).
This morphism makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{2},\rho_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho_{1},\rho_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho_{2},\rho_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{2}),g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho_{1},\rho_{2}),g,r}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{2},r_{2}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}.}

Moreover, if we take
\widetilde{\rho}_{1}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r_{1}}
,
\widetilde{\rho}_{2}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r_{2}}
,
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}
with
\widetilde{\rho}_{1}^{(N)}=\rho_{1}
,
\widetilde{\rho}_{2}^{(N)}=\rho_{2}
, and
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}^{(N)}=\rho_{0}
,
then the same type of gluing procedure (cf.
[
Wak14
, Section 6.4]
) yields a morphism

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}),g,r}

(4.20)

such that the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}},\rho_{0}}
