\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}),g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}),g,r}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{0}),g_{1},r_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{2},\rho_{0}),g_{2},r_{2}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\rho_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho_{1},\rho_{2}),g,r}.}

Next, given nonnegative integers
g
,
r
with
2g+r>0
, we shall write

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r+2}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g+1,r}

(4.25)

for the gluing morphism obtained by attaching the last two marked points of each curve classified by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r+2}
to form a node.

For
\rho\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
and
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}
,
there exists a morphism

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\rho,\rho_{0}}:\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0},\rho_{0}),g,r+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g+1,r}

(4.26)

obtained by gluing each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}
-oper classified by its domain along the fibers over the last two marked points of the underlying curve (cf.
[
Wak14
, Example 6.31, (ii)]
).
This morphism fits into the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0},\rho_{0}),g,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\rho,\rho_{0}}_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho,\rho_{0},\rho_{0}),g,r+2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g+1,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g+1,r}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g+1,r}.}

If we take
\widetilde{\rho}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}
with
\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}=\rho
,
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}^{(N)}=\rho_{0}
, then gluing
p^{N+1}
-nilpotent
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N+1)}
-opers gives rise to a morphism

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g,r+2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g+1,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g+1,r}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0},\rho_{0}),g,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g+1,r}.}

Using the diagrams (
4.3
) and (
4.3
), we prove the following assertion.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let us set
d:=3g-3+r
.
Suppose that
d\geq 1
and that
the following conditions are fulfilled:

(a)

The stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho^{\prime},g^{\prime},r^{\prime}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
for any
\rho^{\prime}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r^{\prime}}
is either empty or irreducible whenever
3g^{\prime}-3+r^{\prime}\leq d
;

(b)

The stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\rho^{\prime},g^{\prime},r^{\prime}}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
for any
\rho^{\prime}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r^{\prime}}
is either empty or connected whenever
3g^{\prime}-3+r^{\prime}<d
.

Also, let
\widetilde{\rho}
be an element of
\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
satisfying
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\neq\emptyset
.
Then, the following assertions hold.

(i)

Any two irreducible components of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
intersect.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is connected.

(ii)

The stack
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is irreducible if it is nonempty.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is smooth over
k
(cf. Theorem
3.8
, (iii)),
the assertion (ii) follows from assertion (i).
Hence, it suffices to prove assertion (i).
For simplicity, we set
\rho:=\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}\in\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
.

First, let us assume that
g\geq 1
.
Since the commutative square diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho_{0},1,1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0}),g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}}\Phi^{\emptyset,\rho,\rho_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}}\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},1,1}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho,\rho_{0}),g-1,r+1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,g,r}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{1,1}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,r}}

is Cartesian and the right-hand vertical arrow is surjective (cf. Theorems
3.8
, (iii)),
there exists an element
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}
satisfying
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho_{0},1,1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0}),g-1,r+1}\neq\emptyset
.

The morphisms
\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}
(cf. (
4.20
)) and
the diagrams (
4.3
)
defined for
(g_{1},g_{2},r_{1},r_{2})=(1,g-1,0,r)
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
fit into the following Cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho}_{0},1,1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\Phi^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0},g,r}\times\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),g-1,r+1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho_{0},1,1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\rho,\rho_{0}),g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{\emptyset,\rho,\rho_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.}

By the condition (b),
the image of
\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}
is connected for any such
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
.
On the other hand,
since
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is irreducible by the condition (a) and

\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}
is finite and faithfully flat (cf. Theorem
3.23
),
the restriction of
\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},g,r}
to each irreducible component of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
is surjective.
Thus, to complete the proof for
g\geq 1
,
it suffices to show that the images of the various morphisms
\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}
’s (for different
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
’s with
\widetilde{\rho}^{(N)}_{0}=\rho_{0}
) are all contained in the same connected component of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
.

Let us take two elements
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
(
i=1,2
) with

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho}_{i},1,1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),g-1,r+1}\neq\emptyset
.
Since
the square diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{\zeta\in\Xi_{2,N}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(\rho_{0},\zeta,\zeta),0,3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\rho_{0},\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\zeta}\Pi^{\prime}_{(\rho_{0},\zeta,\zeta),0,3}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho_{0},1,1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho_{0},1,1}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{1,1}}

is Cartesian and its right-hand vertical arrow is surjective (cf. Theorem
3.8
, (iii)),
one can find an element
\zeta\in\Xi_{2,\Xi}
satisfying
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(\rho_{0},\zeta,\zeta),0,3}\neq\emptyset
, or equivalently,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(\zeta,\zeta,\rho_{0}),0,3}\neq\emptyset
.
Let us take
\widetilde{\zeta}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\zeta)\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
.
Then, it follows from Corollary
3.24
that both
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}_{1}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
and

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
are nonempty.

For
i=1,2
, the square diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\widetilde{\rho}_{i},\widetilde{\zeta}}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta}),\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho}_{i},1,1}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),g-1,r+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}),g-1,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma_{g-1,r+2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta}),g-1,r+2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\zeta}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}}

is commutative,
where
\varsigma_{g-1,r+2}
denotes the isomorphism

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}),g-1,r+2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\zeta}),g-1,r+2}

induced by reordering the marked points of underlying curves.
Since
its upper-left corner is nonempty, we have

\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}})\cap\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\zeta}})\neq\emptyset
.
Moreover, by the condition (b),

\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\zeta}})
is connected.
It follows that
\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{1}})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{\emptyset,\widetilde{\rho},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}})
are contained in the same connected component of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
.
This completes the proof for
g\geq 1
.

Next, assume that
g=0
, which implies
r\geq 4
.
(The following discussion proceeds in essentially the same way as the previous case of
g\geq 1
.)
Let us write
\widetilde{\rho}:=(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\lambda})
for

\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}\in\Xi_{2,N+1}^{r-3}
, and write

\alpha:=\widetilde{\alpha}^{(N)}
,
\beta:=\widetilde{\beta}^{(N)}
,
\gamma:=\widetilde{\gamma}^{(N)}
, and
\lambda:=\widetilde{\lambda}^{(N)}
(hence
\rho=(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\lambda)
).
Since the commutative square diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{0}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\lambda,\rho_{0}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma),\lambda,\rho_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}}\Pi^{\prime}_{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{0}),0,4}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{(\lambda,\rho_{0}),0,r-2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,0,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{\rho,0,r}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,4}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,r}}

is Cartesian and the right-hand vertical arrow is surjective (cf. Theorem
3.8
, (iii)),
there exists
an element
\rho_{0}\in\Xi_{2,N}
satisfying

\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{0}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\lambda,\rho_{0}),0,r-2}\neq\emptyset
.

The morphisms
\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}

defined for
(g_{1},g_{2},r_{1},r_{2})=(0,0,3,r-3)
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
fit into the following Cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}\Phi^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\rho_{0}}\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),0,4}\times\Lambda_{(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}),0,r-2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},0,r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},0,r}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{0}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\lambda,\rho_{0}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,0,r}.}

By the condition (b), the image of
\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}
is connected for any such
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
.
On the other hand, since
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,0,r}
is irreducible by the condition (a) and
\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},0,r}
is finite and faithfully flat (cf. Theorem
3.23
),
the restriction of
\Lambda_{\widetilde{\rho},0,r}
to each irreducible component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},0,r}
is surjective.
Thus, to complete the proof for
g=0
,
it suffices to show that the image of the various morphisms
\Phi_{\mathrm{loop}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{0}}
(for
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
’s with
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}^{(N)}=\rho_{0}
) lie in the same connected component of
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},0,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
.

Let us take two elements
\widetilde{\rho}_{i}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\rho_{0})\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
(
i=1,2
) with
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,r-2}

\neq\emptyset
.
Since the commutative square diagram

\textstyle{\coprod_{\zeta\in\Xi_{2,N}}\mathcal{O}p_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\zeta),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times\mathcal{O}p_{N,(\gamma,\rho_{0},\zeta),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}^{(\alpha,\beta),(\gamma,\rho_{0}),\zeta}}}
\scriptstyle{\coprod_{\zeta}\Pi^{\prime}_{(\alpha,\beta,\zeta),0,3}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{(\gamma,\rho_{0},\zeta),0,3}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{i}),0,4}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi^{\prime}_{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\rho_{0}),0,4}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,4}}

is Cartesian and its right-hand vertical arrow is surjective (cf. Theorem
3.8
, (iii)),
one can find an element
\zeta\in\Xi_{2,N}
satisfying
\mathcal{O}p_{N,(\alpha,\beta,\zeta),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\times\mathcal{O}p_{N,(\gamma,\rho_{0},\zeta),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
.
Let us take
\widetilde{\zeta}\in{{}^{\dagger}}\pi^{-1}(\zeta)\cap\Xi_{2,N+1}
.
Then, it follows from Corollary
3.24
that both

\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\times\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{1},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
and
\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}\times\mathcal{O}p_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{2},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}
are nonempty.

For
i=1,2
, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{i},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}),(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),\widetilde{\zeta}}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times\varsigma_{0,3}\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,4}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\lambda}),0,r-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\zeta}),(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),\widetilde{\zeta}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}\times\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,r-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}),(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\lambda}),\widetilde{\zeta}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},0,r}}

is commutative, where
\varsigma_{0,3}
denotes the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\rho}_{i},\widetilde{\zeta}),0,3}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\zeta},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}),0,3}
induced by reordering the marked points of underlying curves.
Since its upper-left corner is nonempty,
we have
\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{i}})\cap\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}),(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\lambda}),\widetilde{\zeta}})\neq\emptyset
.
Moreover, by the condition (b),

\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta}),(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\lambda}),\widetilde{\zeta}})
is connected.
It follows that
\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{1}})

and
\mathrm{Im}(\Phi_{\mathrm{tree}}^{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\gamma}),\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\rho}_{2}})

are contained in the same connected component of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N+1,\widetilde{\rho},0,r}
.
This completes the proof for
g=0
.
∎

By applying the above result, we conclude the following theorem.

Theorem 4.6

(cf. Theorem
A
)
.

(Recall that we have assumed the equality
n=2
.)
Let
\rho
be an element of
\Xi_{2,N}^{r}
, where we set
\rho:=\emptyset
when
r=0
.
Then, the following assertions hold.

(i)

If
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}\neq\emptyset
, then
any two irreducible components of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
intersect.
In particular,
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{nilp}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
is connected (when it is nonempty).

(ii)

If
\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\neq\emptyset
, then

\mathcal{O}p_{N,\rho,g,r}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is irreducible.

Proof.

We shall set
d:=3g-3+r
.
Assertions (i), (ii) for
N=1
and
d\geq 0
have been established in
[
Moc2
, Chapter II, Theorems 1.12 and 2.8]
.
Assertion (i) for
N\geq 1
and
d=0
follows from Corollary
3.24
.
Moreover, according to Lemma
4.4
, assertion (ii) holds when
N\geq 1
and
d=1
.
The remaining cases can be proved by induction on
N
and
d
, based on these base cases and Lemma
4.5
.
∎

4.4.
The projective system of level-reduction maps

We here present an assertion deduced from the previous theorem, which concerns the process of raising the level of each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper.

Let
\rho:=(\rho_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
be an
r
-tuple of elements in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}:=\varprojlim_{N}\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.
For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we denote by
\rho_{i,N}
the image of
\rho_{i}
via the natural projection
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}
, and write
\rho_{N}:=(\rho_{i,N})_{i=1}^{r}
.
Under the identification
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{N})^{\times}/\{\pm 1\}=\Xi_{2,N}
(cf. Example
3.6
),
the level reductions of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers (for various
N
’s) yield
a projective system of
k
-stacks

\displaystyle\cdots\xrightarrow{\Pi_{N+1\Rightarrow N}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho_{N},g,r}\xrightarrow{\Pi_{N\Rightarrow N-1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{\Pi_{3\Rightarrow 2}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{2,\rho_{2},g,r}\xrightarrow{\Pi_{2\Rightarrow 1}}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1,\rho_{1},g,r}.

(4.71)

Proposition 4.7
.

(Recall that we have assumed the equality
n=2
.)
Let us retain the above notation, and suppose that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho_{N},g,r}\neq\emptyset
for a positive integer
N
.
Then,
the morphism
\Pi_{N^{\prime}\Rightarrow N^{\prime}-1}
for every
N^{\prime}\leq N

in the projective system (
4.71
) is finite,
faithfully flat, and generically étale.

Proof.

The assertion follows from a standard argument using
Theorem
4.6
, (ii), and the various geometric properties of
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
asserted in Theorem
3.8
, (ii) and (iii).
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
S
be a scheme over
k
and
\mathscr{X}
an unpointed stable curve over
S
of genus
g>1
.
Also, let
N
,
N^{\prime}
be positive integers with
N^{\prime}\leq N
and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
.
Then, after possibly changing the base over a finite, faithfully flat, and generically étale morphism
T\rightarrow S
,
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper whose level reduction to
N^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
4.7
and the fact that
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\emptyset,g,0}\neq\emptyset
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Corollary 6.27, (ii)]
).
∎

5.
Applications to certain lifting properties

In this section, we present
several applications of
the surjectivity of the morphisms in (
4.71
), as established in Proposition
4.7
.

Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{g}
the moduli stack classifying smooth proper curves of genus
g>1
over
k
.
Throughout this section,
we fix a curve
X
over
k
classified by
\mathcal{M}_{g}
.

5.1.
Maximally Frobenius-destabilized bundles

We begin by addressing the problem of whether a given vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
admits a Frobenius descent; that is, whether there exists a vector bundle
\mathcal{U}
such that
F_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{U})\cong\mathcal{V}
, or more generally,
F_{X}^{m*}(\mathcal{U})
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
When restricted to the class of stable bundles, this question amounts to knowing the (set-theoretic) image of the
generalized Vershiebung rational map

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ver}^{n}_{X}:SU^{n}_{X}\dashrightarrow SU^{n}_{X}

(5.1)

on the moduli space
SU^{n}_{X}
of rank
n
stable bundles on
X
with trivial determinant (cf.
[
MeSu
, Lemma A]
).
To be precise, this rational map is defined by the assignment
\mathcal{U}\mapsto F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{U})
.
Since the Frobenius pull-back of a stable bundle may not remain stable,
\mathrm{Ver}^{n}_{X}
is not necessarily defined on all of
SU^{n}_{X}
.

Unfortunately,
our current understanding of this rational map is limited, as few general results are known for arbitrary triples
(p,n,g)
.
Relevant studies in specific cases can
be found in
[
LanPa
]
,
[
LasPa2
]
,
[
LasPa3
]
, and
[
Oss
]
.

We now focus on the case
n=2
.
For a rank
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
of degree
0
,
the stability is equivalent to the condition that every line subbundle of
\mathcal{V}
has negative degree.
On the other hand, if
\mathcal{V}
is indecomposable,
the maximal degree of line subbundles is at most
g-1
.
Taking this into account,
we introduce the following definition (cf.
[
JoPa
]
,
[
JRXY
]
,
[
LanPa
]
,
[
Li
]
,
[
Zha
]
for the case
N=1
, and
[
KoWa
]
,
[
Wak11
]
for general
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
).

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be as above, and suppose further that
\mathcal{V}
is stable.
Then, we say that
\mathcal{V}
is
maximally
F^{N}
-destabilized
(where
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
)
if the pull-back
F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V})
admits a line subbundle of degree
g-1
.
(This definition coincides with those in
[
KoWa
]
and
[
Wak11
]
after changing the base of
X
along
F_{\mathrm{Spec}(k)}^{N}
.)
Moreover, we say that
\mathcal{V}
is
maximally
F^{\infty}
-destabilized

if it is maximally
F^{N}
-destabilized for some
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Now, let
B_{1}
be the set of
k
-rational points of
SU^{2}_{X}
classifying all maximally
F^{1}
-destabilized bundles.
If the pull-back
\mathcal{U}:=F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V})
of
\mathcal{V}
is again stable,
then
\mathcal{V}
is maximally
F^{\infty}
-destabilized if and only if
\mathcal{U}
is maximally
F^{\infty}
-destabilized.
Hence, the subset

\displaystyle B_{\infty}:=\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}((\mathrm{Ver}_{X}^{2})^{N})^{-1}(B_{1})\subseteq SU_{X}^{2}(k)

(5.2)

coincides with the set of all maximally
F^{\infty}
-destabilized bundles in
SU_{X}^{2}(k)
.
Since no point of
B_{\infty}
is periodic under the action of
\mathrm{Ver}_{X}^{2}
,
there are infinitely many elements of

B_{\infty}
.

As established in
[
JoXi
, Proposition 3.3]
(see also
[
JRXY
]
,
[
JoPa
]
,
[
KoWa
]
, and
[
Wak12
]
),
the Frobenius pull-back functor yields a bijective correspondence between maximally
F
-destabilized bundles and dormant opers, and moreover this correspondence can be extended to the case of level
N
(cf. the proof of Proposition
5.2
stated below).
As an application of this fact together with the result of the previous section,
we obtain the following statement.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be
a maximally
F^{\infty}
-destabilized bundle classified by
SU_{X}^{2}
.
Then, there exists a vector bundle
\mathcal{U}
classified by
SU^{2}_{X}
with
F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{U})\cong\mathcal{V}
.
In other words,
the set-theoretic image of
\mathrm{Ver}^{2}_{X}
contains the set
B_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Let us take a maximally
F^{N}
-destabilized bundle
\mathcal{V}
classified by
SU_{X}^{2}
(where
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
).
By definition, the pull-back
F_{X}^{N*}(\mathcal{V})
admits
a unique line subbundle
\mathcal{L}
of degree
g-1
.
Denote by
\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
the canonical
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure discussed in
[
Mon
, Corollaire 3.3.1]
.

We claim that
\mathcal{L}
is not preserved under
\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
.
Suppose, on the contrary, that
\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
restricts to a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{L}
.
Since
\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature,
there exists a line subbundle
\mathcal{N}
on
\mathcal{V}
with
F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{N})\cong\mathcal{L}
(cf.
[
LeQu
, Corollary 3.2.4]
).
Its degree satisfies
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{N})=\frac{1}{p^{N}}\cdot\mathrm{deg}(F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{N}))=\frac{1}{p^{N}}\cdot\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{L})=\frac{g-1}{p^{N}}>0
, which contradicts the stability assumption on
\mathcal{V}
.
This completes the proof of the claim.

By this claim, the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq 1}\otimes\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}}F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V})

(5.3)

is injective over the generic point.
By comparing the degrees of the domain and codomain, we see that this composite is an isomorphism.
Thus,
the collection
(F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V}),\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)},\mathcal{L})
forms a dormant
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper, in the sense of
[
Wak14
, Definitions 5.6, 5.7]
.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X}

\displaystyle\cong F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong F^{N*}_{X}(\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V}))\cong\mathrm{det}(F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V}))

(5.4)

\displaystyle\cong(\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{L})\otimes((\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)}/\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}^{(N-1)})\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}.

Hence, the trivial
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
can be transposed into a
\mathcal{D}^{(N)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}

on
\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}
, and
\widetilde{\vartheta}:=(\mathcal{L},\nabla_{\widetilde{\vartheta}})
defines a dormant
2^{(N+1)}
-theta characteristic of
X
.
Let
\nabla_{\vartheta}
denote the
2^{(N)}
-theta characteristic induced by

\nabla_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}
.
Under the identification
F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V})=\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}\left(:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{\leq 1}\otimes\mathcal{L}\right)
given by (
5.3
),
the triple
(F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{V}),\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)},\mathcal{L})
defines a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper.
It follows from Corollary
4.8
and the bijective correspondence (
3.7
)
that there exists a dormant
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N+1)},\widetilde{\vartheta})
-oper
\nabla^{\diamondsuit}
whose
associated
(\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)},\vartheta)
-oper is isomorphic to the above triple.
Let
\mathcal{U}:=\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
, which is endowed with an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure via push-forward along the underlying homeomorphism of
F^{N+1}_{X}
.
Then, there exists an isomorphism
F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{U})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}
.
Since
\mathrm{det}(\nabla^{\diamondsuit})
is trivial,
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{U})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Also, the stability of
\mathcal{V}
implies that
\mathcal{U}
is stable.
Therefore, the vector bundle
\mathcal{U}
satisfies the desired properties, which completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

We now recall
(cf.
[
dSa
, Section 2.2]
)
that an
F
-divided sheaf
on
X
is a collection

\displaystyle\{(\mathcal{E}_{m},\alpha_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

(5.5)

where each pair
(\mathcal{E}_{m},\alpha_{m})
consists of a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{m}
on
X
and an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear isomorphism

\alpha_{m}:F_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m+1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{E}_{m}
.
If we are given an
F
-divided sheaf
\{(\mathcal{E}_{m},\alpha_{m})\}_{m}
,
then the equalities
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{E}_{m})=\mathrm{deg}(F_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m+1}))=p\cdot\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{E}_{m+1})
for every
m
imply that
the degree
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{E}_{0})
of
\mathcal{E}_{0}
must be zero.

It is well-known that
each
F
-divided sheaf associates a group representation of a certain pro-algebraic group, often called the
stratified fundamental group
of
X
(cf.
[
dSa
, Definition 7]
).
Also, such an object corresponds to a structure of
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{(\infty)}
-module on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
(cf.
[
Gie
, Theorem 1.3]
), where
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{(\infty)}
denotes the sheaf of differential operators in the sense of Grothendieck (cf.
[
Gro
, Section 16.8.1]
).
By applying Proposition
5.2
,
we obtain
the following assertion.

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a (stable and) maximally
F^{(\infty)}
-destabilized vector bundle on
X
of rank
2
and degree
0
.
Then,
there exists an
F
-divided sheaf
\{(\mathcal{E}_{m},\alpha_{m})\}_{m}
on
X
satisfying
\mathcal{E}_{0}=\mathcal{V}
.
In other words,
\mathcal{V}
admits a
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{(\infty)}
-module structure extending its
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Proof.

Note that we can find a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
satisfying
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\cong\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{V})
(hence
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee})
is trivial).
Since
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{L})=0
,
the line bundle
\mathcal{L}
admits a structure of
F
-divided sheaf, i.e., there exists an
F
-divided sheaf
\{(\mathcal{L}_{m},\beta_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
with
\mathcal{L}_{0}=\mathcal{L}
.
On the other hand,
by applying Proposition
5.2
inductively to
\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}
,
we obtain an
F
-divided sheaf
\{(\mathcal{F}_{m},\gamma_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
with
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}
.
The tensor product
\gamma_{m}\otimes\beta_{m}
(for
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) defines an isomorphism
\left(F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{F}_{m+1})\otimes F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{L}_{m+1})\cong\right)F^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{F}_{m+1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{m+1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{m}
.
Under the natural identification
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{F}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{L}
,
the collection
\{(\mathcal{F}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{m},\gamma_{m}\otimes\beta_{m})\}_{m}
forms the desired
F
-divided sheaf.
∎

5.2.
Deformations to prime-power characteristic

In this subsection, we study
deformations of flat modules to prime-power characteristic.
Denote by
W_{N}
the ring of Witt vectors of length
N
over
k
(hence
W_{1}=k
).
Let us fix a smooth deformation
f_{N}:X_{N}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(W_{N})
of
X
over
W_{N}
.
Note that, just as in the case of
X/k
discussed in Section
2.1
,
we can define the notion of a connection on an
\mathcal{O}_{X_{N}}
-module.
Moreover, the notion of a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper on
X_{N}
can be formulated in terms of connections, see
[
Wak14
, Definition 5.2, (i)]
.

Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be a flat module on
X_{N}
, i.e., a pair consisting of an
\mathcal{O}_{X_{N}}
-module
\mathcal{F}
and a connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{F}
.
Suppose further that
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle of rank
n
.

Definition 5.4

(cf.
[
Wak14
]
, Definition 3.4)
.

We say that
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
is
dormant
if it is, Zariski locally on
X_{N}
, isomorphic to

(\mathcal{O}_{X_{N}},d)^{\oplus n}
, where
d
denotes the universal derivation on
\mathcal{O}_{X_{N}}
over
W_{N}
.
Moreover, a
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}
-oper on
X_{N}
is said to be
dormant

if it is induced from a dormant flat module of rank
n
via projectivization.

We recall the diagonal reductions of dormant flat modules (cf.
[
Wak14
, Section 3]
)
in the non-logarithmic setting.
Let
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
be as above, and denote by
\mathcal{F}_{1}
the mod
p
reduction of
\mathcal{F}
.
For each open subscheme
U
of
X\left(=k\times_{W_{N}}X_{N}\right)
,
we define
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(U)
to be the set of sections
v\in\mathcal{F}_{1}(U)
admitting a lifting
\widetilde{v}
in
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)(U)
,
where we regard
U
as an open subscheme of
X_{N}
via the underlying homeomorphism of the closed immersion
X\hookrightarrow X_{N}
.
The Zariski sheaf
\mathcal{V}
on
X
associated to the presheaf
U\mapsto\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(U)
is endowed with an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure via push-forward along
F_{X}^{N}
.
The morphism
F_{X}^{N*}(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}
corresponding to the inclusion
\mathcal{V}\hookrightarrow F_{X*}^{N}(\mathcal{F}_{1})
via the adjunction relation “
F^{*}_{X}(-)\dashv F_{X*}(-)
”
turns out to be an isomorphism because of the dormancy assumption.
Through this isomorphism,
\nabla_{\mathcal{V},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
is transferred to a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla
on
\mathcal{F}_{1}
with vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature (cf.
[
Wak14
, Proposition 3.11, (ii)]
).
Thus, we obtain a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-bundle

\displaystyle{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\mathscr{F}:=(\mathcal{F}_{1},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla),

(5.6)

which is called the
diagonal reduction
of
\mathscr{F}
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Definition 3.7]
).

This construction yields a map of sets

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of dormant $\mathrm{PGL}_{n}$-opers on $X_{N}$}\end{matrix}\right)\xrightarrow{}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of dormant $\mathrm{PGL}_{n}^{(N)}$-opers on $X_{1}$}\end{matrix}\right).

(5.7)

According to
[
Wak14
, Theorem D]
, this map becomes bijective
when
n=2
and
X
is sufficiently general in the moduli stack
\mathcal{M}_{g}
(cf.
[
KaWa
, Theorem D]
for the case of elliptic curves).
Using this bijection and the results established so far,
we obtain the following assertion.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
N^{\prime}
be a positive integer with
N^{\prime}\leq N
and
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper on
X_{N^{\prime}}
.
Suppose that
X
is general in
\mathcal{M}_{g}
.
Then,
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
\mathscr{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
on
X_{N}

whose reduction modulo
p^{N^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}
.

In what follows, we reformulate the above result as a statement about deformations of differential operators.
For the precise definitions of the terms involved, we refer the reader to
[
Wak14
, Sections 5.7-5.8]
.

Let
N^{\prime}
be a positive integer with
N^{\prime}\leq N
and
\mathcal{L}
a line bundle on
X_{N}
satisfying
p^{N}\mid 2g-2+2\cdot\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{L})
.
Denote by
\mathcal{L}_{N^{\prime}}

the reduction of
\mathcal{L}
modulo
p^{N^{\prime}}
.
Suppose that we are given a second-order linear differential operator
\mathscr{D}
on
\mathcal{L}_{N^{\prime}}
with unit principal symbol and having a full set of root functions (cf.
[
Wak14
, Definition 5.27, 5.31]
).

Proposition 5.6
.

Let us keep the above notation, and
suppose that
X
is general in
\mathcal{M}_{g}
.
Then,
there exists
a second-order linear differential operator
\mathscr{D}_{N}
on
\mathcal{L}
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

\mathscr{D}_{N}
has unit principal symbol and has a full set of root functions;

(ii)

The mod
p^{N^{\prime}}
reduction of
\mathscr{D}_{N}
coincides with
\mathscr{D}
.

Proof.

Write
\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{X/k}
and
\mathcal{T}_{N^{\prime}}:=\mathcal{T}_{X_{N^{\prime}}/W_{N^{\prime}}}
for simplicity.
Since
X
is assumed to be general, we may assume, without loss of generality, that

X
is ordinary in the usual sense.
There exists a dormant
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic
\vartheta:=(\varTheta,\nabla_{\vartheta})
of
X_{N^{\prime}}
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Definition 5.12]
for the definition of a dormant
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic in prime-power characteristic) such that
\varTheta^{\vee}=\mathcal{L}_{N^{\prime}}

and
\mathscr{D}
defines a
(2,\vartheta)
-projective connection (cf. the proof of
[
Wak14
, Theorem 10.25]
).

Denote by
(\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta})
the diagonal reduction of
(\mathcal{T}_{N^{\prime}}\otimes\varTheta_{N^{\prime}}^{\otimes 2},\nabla_{\vartheta})
.
We regard
\mathcal{S}ol({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta})
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module via push-forward along the underlying homeomorphism of
F_{X}^{N-N^{\prime}}
.
Since
F_{X}^{N-N^{\prime}*}(\mathcal{S}ol({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta}))\cong\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2}
and
p^{N}\mid\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2})
,
the degree of the line bundle
\mathcal{S}ol({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta})

is divisible by
p^{N^{\prime}}
.
Hence, there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{N}
with
F^{N^{\prime}*}_{X}(\mathcal{N})\cong\mathcal{S}ol({{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Leftarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta})
,
which yields an isomorphism
F^{N*}_{X}(\mathcal{N})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2}
.
By this isomorphism,
\nabla_{\mathcal{N},\mathrm{can}}^{(N-1)}
corresponds to a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\vartheta,N}
on
\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2}
.
Then, it follows from
[
Wak14
, Proposition 3.19]

that
(\mathcal{T}\otimes\varTheta_{1}^{\otimes 2},\nabla_{\vartheta,N})
admits a unique diagonal lifting of the form
(\mathcal{T}_{N}\otimes(\mathcal{L}^{\vee})^{\otimes 2},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta,N})
for some connection
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta,N}
.
The mod
p^{N^{\prime}}
reduction of
{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta,N}
coincides with

\nabla_{\vartheta}
.
In particular, the pair
\vartheta_{N}:=(\mathcal{L}^{\vee},{{}^{\rotatebox[origin]{45.0}{$\Rightarrow$}\!}}\nabla_{\vartheta,N})
defines a dormant
2^{(1)}
-theta characteristic of
X_{N}
.

Next, let
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N^{\prime}}
be the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N^{\prime})}
-oper on
X_{1}
obtained as the diagonal reduction of the dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper corresponding to the
(2,\vartheta)
-projective connection
\mathscr{D}
, through the chain of bijections established in
[
Wak14
, Section 5.8]
.
According to Corollary
4.8
,
there exists a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{N}
-oper
\mathscr{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}
on
X
whose level-
N^{\prime}
reduction is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}_{N^{\prime}}^{\spadesuit}
.
This also induces a canonical diagonal lifting over
X_{N}
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Theorem-Definition 9.4]
), which
corresponds to a
(2,\vartheta_{N})
-projective connection
\mathscr{D}_{N}
.
By the uniqueness of canonical diagonal liftings, the mod
p^{N^{\prime}}
reduction of
\mathscr{D}_{N}
must coincide with
\mathscr{D}_{N^{\prime}}
.
Thus,
\mathscr{D}_{N}
satisfies the desired properties, completing the proof of this assertion.
∎

6.
Appendix: Generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers and Igusa-structures

In this appendix, we discuss dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers, which are certain variants of dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers; see
[
KaWa
]
or
[
Wak3
]
for the study of such objects.
We show that dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on an elliptic curve correspond, in a suitable sense, to Igusa-structures of level
p^{N}
on that curve (cf. Proposition
6.6
).
In particular, this correspondence allows us to interpret the moduli stacks
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
(at least, in the case
n=2
)
as higher-genus generalizations of the classical Igusa curves.

Throughout this section, we assume that
p>2
.

6.1.
Dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers

Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
the dense open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{1,1}

classifying smooth curves.
That is,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
is the moduli stack of elliptic curves (equipped with a distinguished point) over
k
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
) denote the open (resp., closed) substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
classifying ordinary (resp., supersingular) elliptic curves; see
[
KaMa
, Chapter 12]
for the study of these moduli spaces.
We also denote by
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}:=(X_{\mathrm{univ}},\{\sigma_{\mathrm{univ}}\})
the universal family of elliptic curves over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}

Let
S
be a scheme over
k
and
\mathscr{X}:=(f:X\rightarrow S,\{\sigma\})
a pointed curve classified by an
S
-rational point of

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
.
The family of curves
X
carries
a structure of group scheme over
S
with
\sigma
taken as the identity section.
To simplify the notation, we set
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}:=\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}_{X/S}
and
\mathcal{T}:=\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.

Remark 6.1
.

Note that many of the constructions on pointed stable curves considered in this paper extend naturally to the case where the hyperbolic condition
2g-2+r>0
is not assumed.
For example, one can still define a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on a proper smooth curve of genus
\leq 1
.
In what follows, when we refer to dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on “
X
” (rather than on the pointed curve “
\mathscr{X}
” as before),
we assume that the underlying curve is equipped with the trivial log structure, in contrast to the framework discussed in Section
2.1
.

According to
[
Wak14
, Proposition 7.2]
(extended to the situation where the condition
2g-2+r>0
is not assumed),
each dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
X
can be identified with a dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
\mathscr{X}
whose radius at the marked point
\sigma
is represented by the multiset
[\overline{0},\overline{1},\cdots,\overline{n-1}]
.

Recall that the algebraic group
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
may be identified with the automorphism group of the projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Under this identification,
we define

\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
to be the algebraic subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
consisting of all automorphisms
h
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
that fix the point at infinity, i.e.,
h(\infty)=\infty
.
Each element of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
may be represented by a matrix of the form
\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
b&c\end{pmatrix}
for some
a,c\in k^{\times}
and
b\in k
.
Moreover, such a representative may be chosen with
a=1
.
We also set
B^{\mathbb{A}}=B\cap\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
.

Let us consider a pair
\hat{\mathscr{E}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}},\phi^{\mathbb{A}})
consisting of a
B^{\mathbb{A}}
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}}
on
X
and an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi^{\mathbb{A}}
on the associated
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{A}}:=\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}}\times^{B^{\mathbb{A}}}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
(with respect to the trivial log structure on
X
); see
[
Wak14
, Definition 2.3]
for the definition of a higher-level PD stratification).
By changing the structure group via the inclusion
B^{\mathbb{A}}\hookrightarrow B

(resp.,
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
),

\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{A}}
(resp.,
\phi^{\mathbb{A}}
) induces a
B
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{B}
(resp., an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi
on
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{B}\times^{B}\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
).

Definition 6.2
.

(i)

We say that
\hat{\mathscr{E}}^{\spadesuit}
is a
generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper

(or a
generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper of level
N
) on
X
if the associated pair
(\mathcal{E}_{B},\phi)
forms a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on
X
.
The notion of an isomorphism between two generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers can also be defined in a natural way.

(ii)

A generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-oper
\hat{\mathscr{E}}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}},\phi^{\mathbb{A}})
is said to be
dormant

if the
p^{N}
-curvature of
\phi^{\mathbb{A}}
(in the sense of
[
Wak14
, Definition 2.10, (ii)]
)
vanishes identically.

Remark 6.3
.

Let us now describe (dormant) generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers in terms of
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-modules (cf.
[
KaWa
, Section 4.3]
).
Consider a collection of data

\displaystyle\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}:=(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{N}),

(6.1)

where
\mathcal{F}
is
a rank
2
vector bundle on
X
,
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
is a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{F}
, and
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{N}
are line subbundles of
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{L}\oplus\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}
is closed under
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
.
Suppose that the composite

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\leq 1}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\otimes\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{F}

(6.2)

is an isomorphism (i.e.,
(\mathcal{F},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}},\mathcal{L})
defines a
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper).
Then, we refer to the collection

\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}

as a
generic Miura
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
on
X
(cf.
[
KaWa
, Definition 4.8, (i)]
).

Let us take a generic Miura
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper.

\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}
as above.
The vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
together with
\mathcal{N}
induces, via the quotient
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
, a
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\mathbb{A}}
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{A}}
on
X
.
The line subbundle
\mathcal{L}
specifies a
B^{\mathbb{A}}
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}}
of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{A}}
.
Since
\mathcal{N}
is preserved by
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
,
we obtain an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\phi^{\mathbb{A}}
on
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{A}}
induced from
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
.
By the assumption that (
6.2
) is an isomorphism,
the resulting pair

\displaystyle\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B^{\mathbb{A}}},\phi^{\mathbb{A}})

(6.3)

forms a generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper (cf.
[
KaWa
, Section 4.7]
).
If
\nabla_{\mathcal{F}}
has vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature, then
\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
is dormant.
Moreover, it is immediately verified that every (dormant) generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper arises in this way from some collection

\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}
.

Denote by
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N}
the set-valued contravariant functor on
\mathcal{S}ch_{/\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}
that assigns, to
each
S
-rational point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
classifying
\mathscr{X}
as above, the set of isomorphism classes of dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers on
X
.
Applying the representability of the stacks
\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N,\rho,g,r}
(cf.
[
Wak14
, Theorem 6.17]
and the last comment in Remark
6.1
),
we see that
\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N}
can be represented by a Deligne-Mumford stack of finite type over
k
.

6.2.
Igusa-structures on elliptic curves

Let
V^{(N)}_{X/S}:X^{(N)}\rightarrow X
denote the dual isogeny to the
N
-th relative Frobenius morphism
F^{(N)}_{X/S}
, which is a cyclic
p^{N}
-isogeny (cf.
[
KaMa
, Lemma 12.2.3]
).

Definition 6.4

(cf.
[
KaMa
]
, Definition 12.3.1)
.

An
Igusa-structure of level
p^{N}
on
X
is an
S
-rational point
P
of
X^{(N)}
that generates the
kernel of
V^{(N)}_{X/S}
.

Denote by
\mathcal{I}g_{N}
the set-valued
contravariant functor on
\mathcal{S}ch_{/\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}
that assigns, to each
S
-rational point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
classifying
\mathscr{X}
as above, the set of Igusa-structures of level
p^{N}
on
X
.
Then,
\mathcal{I}g_{N}
can be represented by a connected smooth Deligne-Mumford stack of dimension
1
over
k
equipped with the finite and faithfully flat morphism
\mathcal{I}g_{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
given by forgetting the data of Igusa-structures (cf.
[
KaMa
, Theorem 12.6.1, Corollary 12.6.2]
).

In what follows, we construct dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-opers by using Igusa-structures of level
p^{N}
.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be as before.

To begin with,
let

\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X/S}
denote the relative Jacobian of
X/S
, which classifies degree-
0
line bundles on
X
rigidified along the section
\sigma
.
There exists an isomorphism of group
S
-schemes
\alpha_{X}:X\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X/S}

given by
x\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{X}(x-\sigma)
.
The base-change of
\alpha_{X}
along
F^{N}_{S}:S\rightarrow S
defines an isomorphism
\alpha_{X^{(N)}}:X^{(N)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X^{(N)}/S}
.
Under the identifications
X=\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X/S}
and
X^{(N)}=\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X^{(N)}/S}
given by
\alpha_{X}
and
\alpha_{X^{(N)}}
, respectively,
the isogeny
V^{(N)}_{X/S}:X^{(N)}\rightarrow X
can be identified with the morphism
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(-):\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X^{(N)}/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X/S}
given by
\mathcal{L}\mapsto F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L})
.

Now, let us fix an Igusa-structure
P
of level
p^{N}
on
X
.
Under the identification
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(-)=V_{X/S}^{(N)}
described above,

P
determines a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X^{(N)}
admitting an isomorphism
\gamma:F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Recall that
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L})
carries a canonical
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure with vanishing
p^{N}
-curvature such that the subsheaf of horizontal sections coincides with

(F^{(N)}_{X/S})^{-1}(\mathcal{L})

(cf.
[
Mon
, Corollaire 3.3.1]
).
This corresponds to a
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure

\displaystyle\nabla_{P}:{{}^{L}}\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{X})

(6.4)

on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
via
\gamma
,
and this construction does not depend on the choice of
\gamma
.

Example 6.5
.

Let us examine the
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{P}
in the case
N=1
, where the underlying curve is taken to be the universal formal deformation of a supersingular elliptic curve.

Let us take a
k
-rational point
q
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
lying on the supersingular locus
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
.
Denote by
S_{0}
the formal neighborhood of
q
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
identified with
S_{0}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[\![t]\!])
, and by

\mathscr{X}_{0}:=(X_{0}/S_{0},\{\sigma_{0}\})

the restriction of the universal curve
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}
over
S_{0}
.
According to
[
KaMa
, Corollary 12.4.4]
,
there exists a nowhere vanishing invariant
1
-form
\omega_{0}
on
X_{0}/S_{0}
satisfying
C_{X_{0}/S_{0}}(\omega_{0})=t\cdot\omega_{0}^{(1)}
, where
C_{(-)}
denotes the Cartier operator of the relative smooth curve “
(-)
” (i.e., the inverse to the operator “
C^{-1}
” discussed in
[
NKat1
, Theorem 7.2]
), and
\omega^{(1)}_{0}
denotes the base-change of
\omega_{0}
along the absolute Frobenius morphism
F_{S_{0}}
.

Now, we set
S:=\mathrm{Spec}(k[\![u]\!])
, where
u:=t^{1/(p-1)}
.
The natural inclusion
k[\![t]\!]\hookrightarrow k[\![u]\!]
gives rise to a
k
-morphism
s:S\rightarrow S_{0}
.
Denote by
\mathscr{X}:=(X/S,\{\sigma\})
the base-change of
\mathscr{X}_{0}
along
s
.
By putting
\omega:=u\cdot s^{*}(\omega_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\omega)=C_{X/S}(u\cdot s^{*}(\omega_{0}))=u\cdot C_{X/S}(s^{*}(\omega_{0}))=u\cdot(t\cdot s^{*}(\omega_{0}^{(1)}))=u^{p}\cdot(s^{*}(\omega_{0}))^{(1)}=\omega^{(1)}.

(6.5)

It follows from
[
NKa2
, Corollary 7.1.3]
that
the connection
\nabla_{\omega}
on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
defined by
\nabla_{\omega}:=d+\omega
has vanishing
p
-curvature.
Under the identification
X^{(1)}=\mathrm{Pic}^{0}_{X^{(1)}/S}
given by
\alpha_{X^{(1)}}
,
the line bundle
\mathcal{S}ol(\nabla_{\omega})
on
X^{(1)}
corresponds to an Igusa-structure of level
p
on
X
.
Hence, it determines an
S
-morphism

\displaystyle S\rightarrow\mathcal{I}g_{1}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}S_{0}.

(6.6)

From the local structure of the projection
\mathcal{I}g_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
established in
[
KaMa
, Corollary 12.6.2, (1)]
,
we deduce that this morphism is an isomorphism.
In particular, if
P
denotes
the universal Igusa-structure over the base-change of
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}
over
\mathcal{I}g_{1}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}S_{0}
,
then the equality

\displaystyle\nabla_{\omega}=\nabla_{P}

(6.7)

holds via the isomorphism (
6.6
).

By using
\nabla_{P}
constructed in (
6.4
), we obtain a collection of data

\displaystyle\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}_{P}:=(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2},\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\nabla_{P},\mathrm{Im}(\Delta),\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{2})),

(6.8)

where
\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}
denotes the trivial
\mathcal{D}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
,
\Delta
denotes the diagonal embedding
\mathcal{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
, and
\iota_{2}
denotes the inclusion into the second factor
\mathcal{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
.

Suppose further that the family of elliptic curves
X/S
is ordinary.
Since
P
restricts to a nonzero point in each fiber of the structure morphism
f:X\rightarrow S
,
the pair of global sections
(1,1)
,
(\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}(1),\nabla_{P}(1))
forms a basis of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
.
It follows that

\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}_{P}
gives rise to a dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper
\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}_{P}

via the construction in Remark
6.3
.
The resulting assignment
P\mapsto\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}_{P}
determines a bijective correspondence

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{The set of Igusa-structures}\\
\text{of level $p^{N}$ on $X$}\end{pmatrix}\xrightarrow{\sim}\begin{pmatrix}\text{The set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of dormant generic Miura}\\
\text{$\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}$-opers on $X$}\end{pmatrix}

(6.9)

(cf.
[
KaWa
, Proposition 4.14]
).
This bijection is functorial with respect to
S
, and thus we obtain the following assertion.

Proposition 6.6
.

(i)

For every
N\geq 1
,
the assignment
(\mathscr{X},P)\mapsto(\mathscr{X},\hat{\mathscr{E}}_{P}^{\spadesuit})
defines
an isomorphism of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
-stacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}g_{N}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{N}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ord}}.

(6.10)

(ii)

In the case
N=1
,
the isomorphism (
6.10
) extends to an isomorphism of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
-stacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}g_{1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1}.

(6.11)

Proof.

Since
assertion (i) follows from the functorial isomorphism (
6.9
), we focus here on proving assertion (ii).

To simplify the notation, we set
S:=\mathcal{I}g_{1}
,
and let

(\mathscr{X},P)
(where
\mathscr{X}:=(X/S,\{\sigma\})
) be the universal object defined over
S
.
Write

\nabla_{P,+}:=\nabla_{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\nabla_{P}
.
Also, denote by
\mathcal{G}
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule of
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
generated by the two global sections
(1,1)
,
\nabla_{P,+}((1,1))\left(=(0,\nabla_{P}(1))\right)
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule
\mathcal{L}
(resp.,
\mathcal{N}
) of
\mathcal{G}
generated by
(1,1)
(resp.,
(0,\nabla_{P}(1))
) specifies a line subbundle of
\mathcal{F}
.

In what follows, we claim that
\mathcal{G}
is closed under
\nabla_{P,+}
, and that the collection
\hat{\mathscr{G}}^{\heartsuit}_{P}:=(\mathcal{G},\nabla_{P,+}|_{\mathcal{G}},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{N})
forms a generic Miura
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(1)}
-oper.
Note that the restriction of
\hat{\mathscr{G}}^{\heartsuit}_{P}
to the base-change
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
of
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}
over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
coincides with
\hat{\mathscr{F}}^{\heartsuit}_{P^{\mathrm{ord}}}
,
where
P^{\mathrm{ord}}
denotes the restriction of
P
to
\mathscr{X}_{\mathrm{univ}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.
Hence, it suffices to examine the fiber over a supersingular curve.
Let
q
be a
k
-rational point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
lying on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}^{\mathrm{ss}}
, and
\widetilde{q}
a unique point of
\mathcal{I}g_{1}
over
q
.
Denote by
U
the formal neighborhood of
\widetilde{q}
in
\mathcal{I}g_{1}
, and identify
U
with
\mathrm{Spec}(k[\![u]\!])
for some formal parameter
u
.
Let
(\mathscr{X}_{U}.P_{U})
, where
\mathscr{X}_{U}:=(X_{U}/U,\{\sigma_{U}\})
, be the restriction of
(\mathscr{X},P)
over
U
.
As discussed in Example
6.5
,
there exists, after possibly replacing the parameter
u
with another, a nowhere vanishing
1
-form
\omega_{0}
on
X_{U}/U
satisfying
\nabla_{P}=d+u\cdot\omega_{0}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{G}|_{X_{U}}
admits a basis

\{(1,1),(0,u)\}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{G}|_{X_{U}}
is closed under
\nabla_{P,+}|_{X_{U}}
, and
the restriction of
\hat{\mathscr{G}}^{\heartsuit}_{P}
to
X_{U}
forms a generic Miura
\mathrm{GL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper.
This
completes the proof of the claim.

Consequently, the assignment
P\mapsto\hat{\mathscr{G}}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}_{P}
(cf. (
6.3
)) defines a morphism
\mathcal{I}g_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p_{1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
extending the isomorphism (
6.10
) in the case
N=1
.
Note that
\mathcal{I}g_{1}
is smooth over
k
(cf.
[
KaMa
, Theorem 12.6.1]
).
On the other hand, by an argument similar to the proof of the smoothness assertion in
[
Wak3
, Theorem 6.3.2]
,

\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{1}
is verified to be smooth over
k
.
Using these facts, we conclude that the morphism
\mathcal{I}g_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p_{1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
just constructed is an isomorphism.
We finish the proof of this assertion.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

As shown in
[
Wak8
, Proposition 5.2.1, (ii)]
,
there exists no dormant generic Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{(N)}
-oper on any supersingular curve when
N\geq 2
.
On the other hand, the fiber of the projection
\mathcal{I}g_{N}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ell}}
over the point classifying any such curve is nonempty (cf.
[
KaMa
, Corollary 12.6.2]
).
Therefore, if
N\geq 2
,
then the isomorphism (
6.10
) cannot extend to an isomorphism between the entire spaces
\mathcal{I}g_{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}\mathcal{O}p_{N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
